**1. Python Files & JS to Modify:**

*   **`…/doctype/business_profile/business_profile.py`**: This file will house the core server-side logic. A new `@frappe.whitelist()` function, `clone_profile`, should be added here.
*   **`…/doctype/business_profile/business_profile_list.js`**: This client-side script is needed to add a "Clone" button to the `Business Profile` list view, handle the user prompt for the new name, and trigger the API call.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**

*   **`Business Profile`**: The primary parent DocType that represents a profile.
*   **Child DocTypes**: The story implies child tables for settings like `Routing Rules`, `Intents`, and `Static Replies`. These would be `Table` fields within the `Business Profile` DocType.
*   **Hooks**: No modifications to `hooks.py` should be necessary; the logic can be self-contained within the DocType's controller and list view script.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**

1.  **Backend (`business_profile.py`):**
    *   Implement `clone_profile(source_name, new_display_name)` decorated with `@frappe.whitelist()`.
    *   Use `frappe.copy_doc('Business Profile', source_name)` to perform a deep copy of the document and all its child table entries (e.g., routing rules, intents).
    *   Set the `display_name` on the newly created doc.
    *   Explicitly nullify sensitive/instance-specific fields on the new doc, such as `whatsapp_auth_token` or `phone_number`, to prevent copying connection state.
    *   Save the new document using `new_doc.save(ignore_permissions=True)` and return its name (`new_doc.name`).

2.  **Frontend (`business_profile_list.js`):**
    *   Use the `page.add_inner_button` or `list_view.add_action` method to add a "Clone" button.
    *   The button's handler should first use `frappe.prompt` to ask the admin for the "New Profile Name".
    *   On confirmation, use `frappe.call` to invoke the `clone_profile` whitelisted method.
    *   On a successful response, navigate the user to the new profile's form view using `frappe.set_route('Form', 'Business Profile', new_profile_name)`.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**

*   **Permissions**: The cloning function should be protected by role checks, but the `doc.save()` call inside may need `ignore_permissions=True` to ensure child table rows are created correctly.
*   **Data Exclusion**: Be rigorous in identifying and clearing all fields that should not be carried over, especially API keys, tokens, or other credentials.
*   **Performance**: The 5-second completion criteria might be challenging if a profile contains thousands of child DocType entries. Test with a large source profile.
*   **User Feedback**: The client-side script must provide clear success (`frappe.msgprint`) or failure (`frappe.throw`) feedback to the admin during the operation.

## Pre-analysis for US-827 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This user story describes a feature for a TypeScript/Node.js application, not a Frappe/Python one. The following technical brief is based on the provided JSON and the project's file structure.

---

### **Technical Brief for US-827: Profile Cloning**

**1. Files to Modify:**
*   **`src/routes/admin/profiles.ts`** (or equivalent): Create a new route handler for the `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone` endpoint. This will validate the request body (`newProfileId`, `displayName`) and call the service layer.
*   **`src/services/profileService.ts`** (hypothetical): A new method, `cloneProfile(sourceId, newProfileData)`, should be added to handle the core business logic, ensuring separation from the routing layer.
*   **`shared/schema.ts`**: The schema definitions for `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` will be used by the ORM (likely Drizzle) to perform the database operations.

**2. Key Components Involved (Node.js/Drizzle equivalent of DocTypes/Hooks):**
*   **API Endpoint:** `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone`. This is the main entry point for the operation.
*   **Database Table:** `rainbow_configs`. The cloning process requires a deep copy of all configuration rows matching the source profile, re-inserting them with the new `profileId`.
*   **Database Table:** `rainbow_kb_files`. This table's rows will also be duplicated for the new profile to link its KB file metadata.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  Implement an admin-only middleware to protect the new API endpoint.
2.  In the `profileService.cloneProfile` method, wrap the logic in a database transaction to ensure atomicity.
3.  Inside the transaction, query all `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` records associated with the `sourceId`.
4.  Map over the query results to create new arrays of records, replacing the `sourceId` with the `newProfileId` in each object.
5.  Use the ORM's bulk insert functionality to efficiently write the new records for both tables.
6.  Return the `newProfileId` in the API response so the UI can navigate to the newly created profile.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Data Integrity:** The entire operation must be atomic. If copying `rainbow_kb_files` fails, the `rainbow_configs` insertion should be rolled back to prevent a corrupt profile.
*   **Exclusions:** Be vigilant about *not* copying sensitive or instance-specific data. The `technicalNotes` explicitly forbid copying WhatsApp authentication state.
*   **Uniqueness Constraint:** The `newProfileId` must be unique. The service should validate this before initiating the transaction to prevent a database error.
*   **Performance:** The acceptance criteria demand completion within 5 seconds. Avoid row-by-row insertions in loops; use bulk database operations instead.


## Iteration 12 - Story: US-842 - Baileys DB auth state health check and corruption recovery at startup

### What was implemented
- `validateAuthState(instanceId, dryRun?)` added to `src/lib/whatsapp/db-auth-state.ts`
  - Queries all rows for the instance from `baileys_auth_state`
  - Verifies JSON parseability of every row value
  - Checks creds row for required fields: noiseKey, signedIdentityKey, registrationId, advSecretKey
  - On corruption (dryRun=false): deletes all rows for the instance, logs WARN
  - On corruption (dryRun=true): reports without deleting (used by health check endpoint)
  - Returns `AuthStateValidationResult` with healthy/credentialsPresent/credentialsValid/signalKeyCount/corruptedKeys/clearedRows
- `notifyAdminAuthStateCorruption(instanceId, clearedRows)` added to `src/lib/admin-notifier.ts`
  - Sends WhatsApp admin notification with re-pair instructions
- `WhatsAppInstance.start()` in `instance.ts` now calls `validateAuthState(this.id)` before `useDbAuthState()`
  - On corruption: logs WARN, fires admin notification (non-blocking)
  - QR pairing flow initiates automatically because corrupted rows are cleared before `makeWASocket`
- `GET /api/rainbow/instances/:id/auth-health` added to `src/routes/admin/whatsapp.ts`
  - Calls `validateAuthState(instanceId, true)` — read-only, no recovery triggered
  - Returns health report JSON

### Patterns discovered
- `validateAuthState` uses `dryRun` flag to support both recovery mode (startup) and read-only mode (health check endpoint)
- Table name in db-auth-state.ts is `baileys_auth_state` with `profile_id` column (not `instance_id`)

### Gotchas
- Admin notifier `notifyAdminAuthStateCorruption` fires async/fire-and-forget at startup (non-blocking)
- Build uses esbuild, passes with 3 pre-existing warnings (no new warnings introduced)

## Pre-analysis for US-827 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
### Technical Brief: Profile Cloning (US-827)

This brief outlines the implementation plan for creating a profile cloning feature within the existing Node.js/TypeScript application.

**1. Python Files to Modify:**
The project is a TypeScript/Node.js application. The primary files to modify will be:
-   **`src/routes/admin/profiles.ts`** (or a similar route handler): To add the new `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone` endpoint.
-   **`src/lib/profileService.ts`** (or a new `cloneProfile.ts` service): To encapsulate the core logic for copying profile data from the source to the new profile. This keeps the route handler clean.
-   **`src/lib/db/schema.ts`** (or `shared/schema.ts`): May require inspection to understand the exact structure of `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` tables.

**2. Frappe DocTypes/Hooks Involved:**
The equivalent components in this architecture are:
-   **API Endpoint:** `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone`
-   **Database Tables (Drizzle ORM):**
    -   `rainbow_configs`: This table will be the primary source for settings like `routing.json`, `knowledge.json`, etc.
    -   `rainbow_kb_files`: Metadata for knowledge base files will be duplicated from this table.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  Define the new route in `src/routes/admin/profiles.ts`. It should validate the request body for `newProfileId` and `displayName`.
2.  The route handler will call a new function, e.g., `cloneProfile(sourceId, newProfileId, displayName)`, in the profile service layer.
3.  Inside `cloneProfile`, use a database transaction (e.g., `db.transaction(async (tx) => { ... })`) to ensure atomicity.
4.  Within the transaction:
    a.  Query all `rainbow_configs` rows where `profileId` matches the `sourceId`.
    b.  Filter out any sensitive or instance-specific configurations (e.g., WhatsApp credentials, phone numbers).
    c.  Map the resulting rows to new objects, replacing the `profileId` with `newProfileId`.
    d.  Perform a batch insert of the new rows into `rainbow_configs`.
    e.  Repeat the query-map-insert process for the `rainbow_kb_files` table.
5.  Return the `newProfileId` in the API response.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
-   **Transactional Integrity:** The entire clone operation must be wrapped in a single database transaction. A failure at any step should roll back all changes to prevent creating a corrupt, partially-cloned profile.
-   **Data Exclusion:** Be vigilant about excluding any data related to the WhatsApp session (`whatsapp-auth/`) or phone numbers from the `settings.json` or other config files. The clone should *never* copy live credentials.
-   **ID Uniqueness:** The backend must verify that the `newProfileId` provided by the client does not already exist before initiating the transaction to prevent a primary key violation.
-   **Non-existent Source:** If the `sourceId` does not exist, the API should return a `404 Not Found` error.
-   **File System:** The user story is explicit: do not copy any files on the disk (e.g., the actual KB documents), only the database metadata records.


## Pre-analysis for US-827 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Based on the user story and your project's file structure, the implementation should be done in **TypeScript/Node.js**, not Frappe/Python. The story describes adding a new API endpoint to an existing Node.js server.

Here is the technical brief for the agent:

**Project:** Rainbow AI (Node.js/TypeScript)
**Goal:** Implement a 'Clone Profile' feature.

**1. Files to Modify:**
*   **Route Definition:** A new route will be needed. Look for a file in `src/routes/` that handles admin or profile-related endpoints, likely `src/routes/admin/profiles.ts` or a similar name.
*   **Core Logic:** Business logic for cloning should be in a dedicated service/library file, perhaps `src/lib/profiles.ts` or `src/services/profileService.ts`.
*   **Database Schema:** Refer to `shared/schema.ts` to understand the structure of the tables you'll be copying.

**2. Key Entities Involved:**
*   **API Endpoint:** `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone`
*   **Database Tables (Drizzle ORM):**
    *   `rainbow_configs`: Main table for profile settings.
    *   `rainbow_kb_files`: Table for knowledge base file metadata.

**3. Implementation Approach:**
1.  In your routes file, add the new `POST` endpoint. It should be protected by an admin authentication middleware.
2.  The route handler will call a new function, e.g., `cloneProfile(sourceId, newProfileId, displayName)`.
3.  This function should use a Drizzle database transaction (`db.transaction(async (tx) => { ... })`).
4.  Inside the transaction:
    *   Fetch all `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` records for the `sourceId`.

    *   Bulk-insert the new records for both tables.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Atomicity:** Use a database transaction to ensure the clone is an all-or-nothing operation.
*   **Error Handling:** Return a 404 if the `sourceId` doesn't exist. Return a 409 if the `newProfileId` is already in use.
*   **Exclusions:** Be vigilant about not copying sensitive or instance-specific data. The `whatsapp-auth/` directory and phone number settings are explicitly excluded.
*   **Data Integrity:** This is purely a database operation. Do not copy or modify any files on the disk (like the KB files themselves).

## Iteration 9 - Story: US-854 - Out-of-stock graceful handling
### What was implemented
- Added `available` field to `DisambiguationCandidate` interface in `disambiguation-store.ts`
- Updated `normalizeItems()` in `fnb-menu.ts` to parse `available` boolean from FnB MCP response
- Updated `cart_search_item` handler in `cart.ts` with out-of-stock detection:
  - When a single match is found with `available: false`, apologises and fetches 2-3 alternatives from same category
  - Alternatives are stored as disambiguation state so guest can pick one via `cart_pick_item`
  - If no alternatives exist in the category, offers to show full menu
  - When multiple matches exist, filters out unavailable items (falls back to all if none available)
- Added OUT-OF-STOCK handling rules to AI waiter system prompt in `webchat-api.ts`

### Patterns discovered
- FnB MCP response items may include `available` boolean — `normalizeItems()` already handles flexible field mapping
- `fetchMenuItems(category)` already supports category filtering, perfect for fetching same-category alternatives
- The disambiguation flow (store → pick) provides a natural UX for suggesting alternatives

### Gotchas
- `available` field is optional — when FnB MCP doesn't return it, items are treated as available (no false positives)
- The `cart_add_item` tool doesn't check availability (by design — it's for exact names from the AI). The check is in `cart_search_item` where menu items are fetched.

*   No Frappe hooks are involved. The trigger is a new Express.js API route.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
*   Define the new route in `src/routes/admin/profiles.ts` to call a new controller/service function `cloneProfile`.
*   The `cloneProfile` service function should wrap all database operations in a Drizzle transaction (`db.transaction(...)`) to ensure atomicity.
*   Inside the transaction:
    1.  Fetch all `rainbow_configs` for the `sourceId`.
    2.  Deep-copy the results, mapping each record to the `newProfileId` and filtering out sensitive/instance-specific settings (e.g., WhatsApp credentials, phone number).
    3.  Perform a bulk insert of the new config records.
    4.  Repeat the select/map/bulk-insert process for the `rainbow_kb_files` metadata table.
    5.  Create the primary profile record in the `rainbow_profiles` table.
*   The controller should return a JSON object `{ "profileId": newProfileId }` on success.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Atomicity:** The entire operation must be wrapped in a single database transaction. If any step fails, the entire clone should be rolled back.
*   **Uniqueness:** The endpoint must validate that the requested `newProfileId` doesn't already exist before starting the transaction to prevent a 409 Conflict.
*   **Exclusion:** Do not copy the actual KB files from the filesystem. The story only requires copying the database metadata rows. Explicitly exclude any WhatsApp authentication-related records from `rainbow_configs`.
*   **Error Handling:** Return a `404 Not Found` if the `sourceId` does not exist.

This technical brief adapts the provided user story, which appears to be for a Node.js/TypeScript environment, into a plan for a Frappe/Python implementation.

### **Technical Implementation Brief for Frappe**

1.  **Files to Modify:**
    *   `lhdn_payroll_integration/api.py`: Create a new whitelisted controller method here, e.g., `clone_profile_config`.
    *   `lhdn_payroll_integration/hooks.py`: Expose the new `clone_profile_config` method as a whitelisted API endpoint (`api_method`).

2.  **Frappe DocTypes / Hooks Involved:**
    *   **Primary DocType:** A main "Business Profile" or "Company Profile" DocType that serves as the parent container.
    *   **Child DocTypes:** Child table DocTypes linked to the parent profile, likely holding settings for "Routing Rules", "Intents", and "KB Files".
    *   **API Hook:** The `api_method` in `hooks.py` will define the `POST` route (`/api/method/lhdn_payroll_integration.api.clone_profile_config`).

3.  **Recommended Approach:**
    *   Implement a new `@frappe.whitelist()` function `clone_profile_config` that accepts `source_profile` (the name of the DocType to clone) and `new_profile_name`.
    *   Use `frappe.copy_doc(source_profile)` which is the idiomatic way to create a deep copy of a document, including its child table entries.
    *   After the copy is created, programmatically update the new document's name/title to `new_profile_name`.
    *   Identify and nullify any fields that should not be carried over, such as authentication tokens or specific external service linkages (e.g., WhatsApp auth state).
    *   Save the modified new document. The function should return the name of the newly created profile document.

4.  **Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
    *   **Permissions:** The API method must handle permissions correctly, likely running with admin rights or using `ignore_permissions=True` when saving the new document.
    *   **Unique Naming:** Ensure the `new_profile_name` doesn't already exist. The code should gracefully handle a naming collision.
    *   **Data Integrity:** The copy operation should be atomic. Use a `try/except` block with `frappe.db.commit()` and `frappe.db.rollback()` to prevent creating partial/corrupt profiles if an error occurs.
    *   **File Data:** The story mentions not copying file content. `copy_doc` will only duplicate the DocType metadata (e.g., a record in the `File` DocType), not the physical file on disk, which aligns with the requirement.


## Iteration 11 - Story: US-843 - Structured data notice on first WhatsApp interaction

- The consent module uses SHA-256 hashed JIDs for PDPA compliance (no raw PII stored)
- Profile-specific knowledge must use `profileConfig.getKnowledge()` not the global `getStaticReply()` which only reads the default configStore

### Gotchas
- `hasConsent()` must be checked BEFORE `recordConsent()` is called — `recordConsent` adds to the in-memory cache synchronously after DB write, but the flag capture happens before the async call starts
- If consent cache fails to load on startup, `hasConsent()` returns false for everyone — but this is acceptable since it's a one-time notice (not harmful to re-send)
- Other profile data directories (data-southern, data-makan, data-pms-*) don't have the notice entry; operators can add it to their profile's knowledge.json as needed

*   **Database Schema:** The relevant table schemas are likely defined in `shared/schema.ts`. You will interact with the `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` tables.
*   **Service Logic:** Business logic for the cloning operation could be placed in a service file, e.g., `src/lib/profiles.ts`.

**2. Key Data Structures (Instead of DocTypes):**
*   **`rainbow_configs` table:** This table stores the core profile settings (like `routing.json`, `settings.json` mentioned in the story). This is your primary source for cloning.
*   **`rainbow_kb_files` table:** This contains metadata about knowledge base files associated with a profile.

**3. Implementation Approach:**
*   Your new API endpoint will receive the `sourceId` from the URL and `{ newProfileId, displayName }` in the request body.
*   Use a database transaction to ensure atomicity.
*   Query all records from `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` where the `profile_id` matches the `sourceId`.
*   For each record, create a new object, replace the `profile_id` with `newProfileId`, and insert it back into the respective table.
*   The endpoint should return the `newProfileId` upon successful completion.

*   Use a transaction to prevent creating partial profiles if an error occurs mid-clone.
*   Handle errors gracefully: return a `404` if the `sourceId` does not exist and a `409` if the `newProfileId` is already taken.
*   The story mentions JSON files, but technical notes and recent migrations point to the database as the source of truth. Prioritize the database tables (`rainbow_configs`).



## Iteration 12 - Story: US-827 - Profile configuration cloning
### What was implemented
- Added `POST /api/rainbow/profiles/:sourceId/clone` endpoint in `src/routes/admin/profiles.ts`
- Endpoint accepts `{ newProfileId, displayName }` body and validates inputs (format, uniqueness)
- Clones all 7 config files (knowledge, intents, templates, settings, workflow, workflows, routing) from source to new profile:
  - DB: copies `rainbow_configs` rows with new `dbConfigPrefix` key prefix in a transaction
  - Disk: copies JSON files from source `dataDir` to new `src/assistant/data-{newProfileId}/`
- Strips WhatsApp-specific fields (`whatsappInstanceId`, `whatsappPhoneNumber`) from cloned settings
- Also copies `kb-patterns.json` if present in source data dir
- Creates empty KB directory at `.rainbow-kb-{newProfileId}/`
- Adds new profile entry to `profiles.json` with generated paths and empty instanceIds
- Returns 201 with the new profileId, list of copied files, and paths

### Patterns discovered
- Profile DB config keys use `dbConfigPrefix:filename` format (e.g., `southern:knowledge.json`), with default pelangi having empty prefix (just `knowledge.json`)
- `ProfilesFile` type is already exported from `profile-registry.ts` — reusable for reading/writing profiles.json
- `pool` from `src/lib/db.ts` can be used directly for raw SQL in admin routes
- New profiles require server restart to be picked up by `ProfileRegistryClass.init()`

### Gotchas
- The default pelangi profile has empty `dbConfigPrefix`, so its config keys are bare filenames (no prefix). All other profiles use `prefix:filename` format. The clone endpoint always uses the new profileId as the prefix.
- `rainbow_kb_files` table has a flat namespace (no profile prefix) — KB files are loaded per-profile from disk `kbDir`. The clone creates an empty kbDir; operators must populate KB content separately.
- Using `ON CONFLICT (key) DO NOTHING` for DB inserts prevents overwriting if a partial clone is retried.

This brief outlines the implementation plan for creating an admin UI for WhatsApp rate-limiting settings within the `lhdn_payroll_integration` Frappe app.

**1. Python Files to Modify:**

*   **New File:** `lhdn_payroll_integration/doctype/whatsapp_rate_limit_rule/whatsapp_rate_limit_rule.py` - To hold server-side logic for the new DocType.
*   **New File:** `lhdn_payroll_integration/api.py` - To define the new whitelisted API endpoint for fetching rate-limit statistics.
*   **Existing File (to be identified):** The Python file containing the current rate-limit enforcement logic needs to be updated to read values from the new DocType instead of its current configuration source.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**

*   **New DocType (Child):** Create a child DocType named `WhatsApp Rate Limit Rule` with the following fields:
    *   `enabled` (Check, default: 1)
    *   `max_messages_per_user_per_24h` (Int, default: 20)
*   **Modified DocType (Singleton):** Modify an existing singleton settings DocType (e.g., `LHDN Payroll Settings` or a similar central configuration DocType). Add a "Table" field named `rate_limit_rules` that uses `WhatsApp Rate Limit Rule` as its source. This will serve as the primary admin UI.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**

1.  Use `bench new-doctype "WhatsApp Rate Limit Rule"` to create the child table DocType. Mark it as a child table in its settings.
2.  Add the `rate_limit_rules` Table field to the main settings DocType via "Customize Form".
3.  Refactor the existing rate-limiting enforcement logic to query the new `WhatsApp Rate Limit Rule` table for its configuration.
4.  In `api.py`, create a `@frappe.whitelist()` function `get_rate_limit_stats`. This function will query the relevant message/log DocType, counting distinct users who were blocked in the last 24 hours.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**

*   **Caching:** Frappe aggressively caches DocType settings. Ensure the enforcement logic is not using stale cached values after an admin saves changes. Use `frappe.db.get_single_value` or clear the relevant cache key upon update.
*   **Performance:** The stats-gathering query could be slow. It must be optimized to efficiently scan logs from the last 24 hours only. Consider adding a DB index on the timestamp and user fields of the logged data.
*   **Default Values:** Ensure that if no rule is defined, the system falls back to a sensible default behavior and does not block all messages.


## Iteration 4 - Story: US-866 Malay-language ordering
### What was implemented
- Enhanced `order_placement` regex patterns in `data-makan/intents.json` with Malay phrases (saya nak, boleh bagi, satu, tolong bagi, minta, tapau, bawa balik, bungkus) and Manglish (can I have, I want lah, add one more lah)
- Added `order_placement`, `menu_query`, and `food_recommendation` intents to `data-makan/intent-keywords.json` with Malay/Manglish keywords for T2 fuzzy matching
- Added comprehensive Malay/Manglish ordering section to system prompt in `webchat-api.ts` covering: add-to-cart, remove, quantity (satu/dua/tiga), special instructions mapping (kurang manis → less sugar, pedas sikit → a little spicy, tanpa bawang → no onion, etc.), takeaway, done ordering, and confirmation phrases
- Updated cart tool descriptions with Malay/Manglish phrase examples
- Extended `cart_set_table` handler to recognize "bawa balik" and "bungkus" as takeaway order types
- Created 23 Vitest tests in `malay-ordering.test.ts` covering: intent pattern matching (5 Malay, 3 Manglish), takeaway detection (3), keyword matching (5), cart handler behavior (3 takeaway, 3 special instructions), and end-to-end Malay flow (1)
### Patterns discovered
- The `unknown` intent routes to `llm_reply`, so even if Malay phrases don't match specific patterns they still reach the LLM — but proper classification ensures correct routing
- Intent keywords (T2) and intent patterns (regex) are separate classification tiers
- The system prompt in `webchat-api.ts` is the most impactful place for Malay support since it directly instructs the LLM on phrase mapping
- Malay quantity words (satu, dua, tiga) need to be regex-matched as order_placement, not just keywords
### Gotchas
- JSON import in tests requires `assert { type: 'json' }` syntax for ESM
- The `intents.json` regex for order_placement is broad (e.g. `nak + word`) — careful not to clash with other intents like check-in ("nak check in")

    *   `rate_limiting_enabled`: (Check)
    *   `max_outbound_per_day`: (Int)

**3. Implementation Approach:**

## Iteration N - Story: US-877 Allergen and dietary warning display on item selection

### What was implemented

  - `cart_search_item`: same check before adding single unambiguous match to cart
  - `cart_pick_item`: same check before adding selected item from disambiguation list
  - `cart_allergen_confirm` new handler: retrieves pending item, adds to cart, clears pending state
- `cart_allergen_confirm` tool definition added to `cartTools` array
- `src/routes/admin/index.ts` — `menu-allergens` route registered (was written but not mounted)
- `src/index.ts` — `initAllergenStore()` called at startup
- `src/assistant/data-makan/allergens.json` — sample data was already present

### Patterns discovered
- allergen-store.ts and menu-allergens.ts were already fully implemented from a prior session but never wired up (import missing from admin index, initAllergenStore never called)
- ESM import must be at top of file — cannot use inline `import` in the middle of execution flow
- allergen-pending-store follows same TTL + interval cleanup pattern as disambiguation-store

### Acceptance criteria coverage
1. allergens[] and dietary_flags[] per item — served from allergens.json via allergen-store ✓
2. Bot displays formatted allergen notice before cart add when data exists ✓

## Pre-analysis for US-844 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This user story is for a Node.js/TypeScript project, not a Frappe/Python app. The technical notes and file paths (`src/index.ts`, `crypto.timingSafeEqual`) point to a JavaScript environment, which matches the `rainbow-ai` project structure.

Here is the technical brief based on the provided JSON for the `rainbow-ai` (Node.js/TypeScript) project:

**Project:** rainbow-ai (Node.js/TypeScript)
**Subject:** Implement Meta Webhook HMAC Signature Validation

**1. Files to Modify:**
*   `src/routes/webhooks/index.ts`: To apply the new validation middleware to the `/webhooks/meta` route.
*   Create `src/lib/middleware/validateMetaSignature.ts`: To house the core validation logic, keeping the routes file clean.
*   `.env.example`: Add `META_APP_SECRET` to inform developers of the required environment variable.

**2. Framework/Concepts Involved:**
*   **Express/Fastify Middleware:** The core pattern is a middleware function that intercepts and validates the request before it reaches the route handler.
*   **Node.js `crypto` module:** Essential for performing the HMAC-SHA256 hashing and constant-time comparison.

**3. Implementation Approach:**
*   The `validateMetaSignature` middleware should read the `X-Hub-Signature-256` header.
*   If the `META_APP_SECRET` env var is missing, log a warning and skip validation.
*   If the header is missing, reject with 403.
*   Compute the HMAC-SHA256 signature of the request's raw body (`req.rawBody`) using the app secret.
*   Use `crypto.timingSafeEqual` to securely compare the computed signature against the one from the header.
*   If validation fails, log a `WARN` with the source IP and return 403.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   The header value is prefixed with `sha256=`. This must be stripped before comparison.
*   The raw request body must be a Buffer. The story confirms `req.rawBody` is already available, which simplifies this.
*   Comparison must be constant-time to prevent timing attacks; a simple `==` is not secure.
*   The absence of the `META_APP_SECRET` environment variable must be handled gracefully by skipping validation, as per requirements.

## Pre-analysis for US-844 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Here is a technical brief for implementing the user story in a Frappe/Python environment.

**Project:** `lhdn_payroll_integration`
**User Story:** US-844 - Webhook HMAC Signature Validation

**Technical Brief for Claude**

This user story describes a webhook validation task but provides technical notes for a Node.js/TypeScript stack. This brief translates the requirements for the Frappe/Python context.

**1. Files to Modify:**
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/api.py`: This is the most likely location for the webhook endpoint logic. Look for a function decorated with `@frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=True)`.
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/hooks.py`: Check for the `api_endpoint` hook to confirm the function handling the `POST /webhooks/meta` route.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**
*   **Whitelist Decorator:** The core mechanism will be the `@frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=True)` decorator on the webhook handler function.
*   **DocTypes:** The webhook likely updates a custom DocType. Investigate DocTypes such as `WhatsApp Message` or `Webhook Log` for storing incoming data.
*   **Site Config:** The `META_APP_SECRET` should be stored in `site_config.json` and accessed via `frappe.conf`.

**3. Implementation Approach:**
1.  In the webhook handler function in `api.py`, retrieve the raw request payload using `frappe.request.data`.
2.  Fetch the signature from the `X-Hub-Signature-256` header via `frappe.request.headers`. Remember to parse the `sha256=` prefix.
3.  Use Python's `hmac` and `hashlib` libraries to compute the expected signature: `hmac.new(app_secret.encode(), frappe.request.data, hashlib.sha256).hexdigest()`.
4.  Use `hmac.compare_digest()` for a constant-time comparison against the received signature.
5.  If validation fails, use `frappe.log_error` to log the failure and source IP (`frappe.request.remote_addr`), then `frappe.throw` with an HTTP 403 status code.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Missing Secret:** If `frappe.conf.get('meta_app_secret')` is missing, log a warning and bypass validation as required.
*   **Raw Body Integrity:** Ensure `frappe.request.data` is the unmodified raw body. Frappe can auto-parse JSON, which would break the signature. If it is parsed, you may need to access the raw data from the underlying Werkzeug request object.
*   **Unit Testing:** Use the Frappe test runner to create a test case that mocks HTTP requests with valid, invalid, and missing `X-Hub-Signature-256` headers.

## Pre-analysis for US-844 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This technical brief translates the provided Node.js user story into a plan for a Frappe/Python environment.

**Project:** `lhdn_payroll_integration`
**Objective:** Implement HMAC-SHA256 signature validation for Meta WhatsApp webhooks.

---

**1. Python Files to Modify:**
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/api.py`: Create this file if it doesn't exist. It's the best practice location for the webhook endpoint logic.
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/hooks.py`: Not strictly necessary for modification if using a standard API endpoint, but would be used if a custom URL route is desired.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**
*   **Endpoint:** The webhook will be a whitelisted Python function, exposed via the Frappe API. e.g., `POST /api/method/lhdn_payroll_integration.api.handle_meta_webhook`.
*   **DocTypes:** The payload data (status updates, receipts) will likely be logged against a DocType such as `WhatsApp Message` or a new custom DocType like `Meta Webhook Log`.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  Define a function `handle_meta_webhook` in `lhdn_payroll_integration/api.py`.
2.  Decorate it with `@frappe.whitelist(allow_guest=True)` to make it a public endpoint. Frappe automatically bypasses CSRF checks for guest-allowed methods.
3.  Retrieve the App Secret from `frappe.conf.get('meta_app_secret')`. If it's missing, log a startup warning and skip validation.
4.  Inside the function, get the raw payload with `frappe.request.data` and the signature from the `X-Hub-Signature-256` request header.
5.  Use Python's `hmac` and `hashlib` libraries to compute the expected signature: `hmac.new(secret, frappe.request.data, hashlib.sha256).hexdigest()`.
6.  Securely compare the received signature with the computed one using `hmac.compare_digest()`.
7.  If invalid, log a warning with the source IP (`frappe.local.request_ip`) and set `frappe.response.http_status_code = 403` before returning.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Configuration:** The `META_APP_SECRET` must be stored as `meta_app_secret` in the site's `site_config.json`.
*   **Whitelist:** `allow_guest=True` is mandatory, as the request originates from Meta's servers without a Frappe user session.
*   **Header Parsing:** The signature header format is `sha256=<hex-digest>`. You must split the string to isolate the hex digest before comparison.
*   **Data vs. Form Dict:** Use `frappe.request.data` to get the raw request body. Do not use `frappe.form_dict` as it will contain the parsed body, which invalidates the HMAC signature.


## Iteration 7 - Story: US-844 - Webhook HMAC signature validation for incoming Meta Cloud API events
### What was implemented
- Added `validateMetaSignature()` middleware to `src/lib/webhook-signature.ts` that uses `META_APP_SECRET` env var
- Returns HTTP 403 (not 401) on invalid/missing signature — distinct from the general webhook guard
- Logs all rejections at WARN level with source IP for security auditing
- If `META_APP_SECRET` is not set, validation is skipped with a startup WARNING log (permissive mode)
- Applied the Meta-specific guard to all 3 Meta webhook routes: `/webhooks/meta/quality`, `/webhooks/meta/account`, `/webhooks/meta/template-status`
- Added 6 unit tests covering: valid signature passes, missing header returns 403, tampered body returns 403, wrong secret returns 403, permissive mode when no secret, source IP logging on rejection

### Patterns discovered
- The project already had a general `validateWebhookSignature` using `WEBHOOK_SECRET` — Meta needs its own secret since Meta's Cloud API uses the app secret, not a custom webhook secret
- `req.ip` and `req.socket.remoteAddress` are available for source IP logging
- The existing test helpers (`makeMocks`) pattern was reusable — created `makeMetaMocks` with additional `ip` and `socket` fields

### Gotchas
- RTK (`rtk npm run build`) failed on this system — `rtk` command not found for npm. Used `npm run build` directly.

1.  Use `vi.mock` to create a mock of the `ai-provider-manager.ts` module.
2.  Mock the Express `req` and `res` objects (`res.write`, `res.end`, etc.) to spy on the SSE stream output.
3.  **Test 1 (Happy Path):** Mock `chatWithFallback` to return a successful text response. Assert `res.write` is called with the expected content.
4.  **Test 2 (Failure Path):** Mock `chatWithFallback` to return a null response. Assert `res.write` is called with the `STREAM_FALLBACK` constant.
5.  **Test 3 (Tool Call Path):** Mock `chatWithFallback` to return tool calls, then a final text response. Assert tool handlers are triggered and the final text is streamed.

**Gotchas to Watch For:**
*   Ensure all external dependencies, especially AI providers and HTTP clients, are fully mocked. The test must not make real network calls.
*   The streamed response must be in the correct Server-Sent Events (SSE) format (e.g., `data: ...\n\n`).
*   Validate that errors are caught and result in a fallback message stream, not a thrown exception that crashes the process.


## Iteration N - Story: US-888 - Vitest AI waiter connectivity regression test
### What was implemented
- Created `src/assistant/__tests__/ai-waiter-connectivity.test.ts` with 3 tests:
  1. Happy path: chatWithFallback returns text → streamed as SSE token
  2. All-providers-fail: SSE response contains STREAM_FALLBACK text, no crash
  3. Tool call path: tool handler invoked, final text streamed
- All 3 tests pass in `npm run test:run`
### Patterns discovered
- `streamChatWithTools` does NOT call `setupSSEHeaders` when chatWithFallback returns text directly (no tool calls) — it goes straight to `sseEvent(res, ...)` → `res.write`. Only `streamFromProviders` calls `setupSSEHeaders`.
- With getProviders() returning [] (mocked), streamFromProviders emits STREAM_FALLBACK immediately.
- The `toolCalls: []` (empty array) is treated the same as no tool calls — the `!toolCalls || toolCalls.length === 0` check handles both.
### Gotchas
- Initial test had incorrect assertion that `res.setHeader` would be called for Content-Type in the happy path — it's only set by `setupSSEHeaders` which is in `streamFromProviders`, not the direct-content path.
## Iteration 1 - Sun, Mar 15, 2026  9:19:11 AM

## Pre-analysis for US-889 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This technical brief is based on the provided JSON user story. Note that the story describes a TypeScript/Vitest task, not a Frappe/Python one as mentioned in the prompt.

**Project:** TypeScript/Node.js application
**Testing Framework:** Vitest

**Technical Brief for US-889**

*   **Primary Files:**
    *   Test implementation: `src/assistant/__tests__/ai-waiter-connectivity.test.ts`
    *   Code under test (contains the fix): `src/assistant/chat-stream.ts`

*   **Key Functions & Modules:**
    *   `streamChatWithTools()`: The main function being tested.
    *   `chatWithFallback()`: The core dependency to be mocked and monitored.
    *   `streamFromProviders()`: The function that should *not* be executed in this scenario.
    *   `sseEvent()`: The event emitter for streaming the response.

*   **Implementation Approach:**
    1.  Add a new test case in `ai-waiter-connectivity.test.ts`.
    2.  Use `vi.spyOn` to mock `chatWithFallback` to return `{ content: '...', toolCalls: undefined }`.
    3.  Create another spy on `streamFromProviders` to track its calls.
    4.  Invoke `streamChatWithTools` and await its completion.
    5.  Assert that the `chatWithFallback` spy was called exactly once.
    6.  Assert that the `streamFromProviders` spy was never called.

*   **Gotchas to Watch For:**
    *   Ensure proper mock setup and teardown (`vi.restoreAllMocks()`) to avoid test pollution.
    *   The spy on `streamFromProviders` may require accessing an internal, un-exported function, which can be tricky.


## Iteration N - Story: US-889 - No redundant second provider call when content is returned
### What was implemented
- Added Test 4 to `src/assistant/__tests__/ai-waiter-connectivity.test.ts`
- Mocks `chatWithFallback` returning `{ content: '...', toolCalls: undefined }`
- Asserts `chatWithFallback` called exactly once (no second round-trip)
- Asserts content streamed as SSE token via `res.write`
- Asserts `getProviders` NOT called (proxy for `streamFromProviders` not being invoked)
- Imported `getProviders` from mock to enable the spy assertion

### Patterns discovered
- `getProviders()` is the first call in `streamFromProviders` — spying on it is a reliable proxy for detecting whether `streamFromProviders` ran
- `toolCalls: undefined` is treated identically to `toolCalls: []` by the `!toolCalls || toolCalls.length === 0` guard
- All 4 tests pass in 12ms total

### Gotchas
- Need to import the mocked function (`getProviders`) explicitly even if it's already mocked at module level
- `vi.mocked()` wrapper needed to get proper TypeScript types for the mock

## Pre-analysis for US-845 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Based on the user story for **US-845**, here is a technical brief for the implementation agent.

**Note:** The user story specifies a Node.js/TypeScript environment (`.ts` files), not Frappe/Python. This brief follows the provided technical notes.

### Technical Brief: WhatsApp Per-Message Cost Tracking

**1. Python Files to Modify (Node.js/TypeScript Equivalents):**
*   `src/routes/admin/whatsapp-cost.ts`: Main logic file for cost calculation. Add new `per-message` calculation logic here.
*   `src/services/whatsapp-sender.ts` (or similar): The core message sending service where the cost increment needs to be triggered upon each successful outbound message.
*   `src/types/profile-settings.ts` (or similar): Define the new `billing.pricingModel` type.
*   `src/routes/admin/dashboard.tsx` (or similar frontend component): Update the UI to display the new cost model data.
*   `src/assistant/__tests__/whatsapp-cost.test.ts`: Add unit tests for the per-message pricing model.

**2. Frappe DocTypes/Hooks (Node.js Equivalents):**
*   **Configuration:** The change centers on a new setting in the profile-specific `settings.json`.
*   **Function Hook:** The "hook" will be a call to a new cost-incrementing function from within the primary outbound message-sending method.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  Extend the settings interface to include `billing: { pricingModel: 'per_conversation' | 'per_message' }`.
2.  In `whatsapp-cost.ts`, create a new function to calculate costs based on message counts and categories (marketing/utility).
3.  Modify the core message-sending function to determine the message category and increment a persistent counter (e.g., in a database or Redis) after each message is sent, but only if `pricingModel` is `'per_message'`.
4.  Update the admin dashboard's data endpoint to provide both cost estimates. The frontend should visually distinguish between the active and inactive models.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   Ensure a default of `'per_conversation'` is handled gracefully for profiles without the new setting.
*   The "proactive = marketing" rule is a heuristic. The system should be designed to accommodate more explicit message categorization in the future.
*   Hardcoded MYR currency rates should be easily configurable, ideally within `settings.json`, to handle future price changes.

- Webchat detection: `phone.startsWith('webchat-')` pattern used throughout codebase
- Interactive payload: set `state.interactivePayload` in action-dispatch; `response-processor.ts` sends it via `sendWhatsAppInteractiveMessage`
- `buildListMessage` enforces 10 TOTAL rows across all sections (not 10 per section)

## Iteration 3 - Story: US-876 POS webhook receiver: push confirmed orders to kitchen display system

### What was implemented
- Created `src/lib/kds-webhook.ts` — KDS/POS webhook dispatcher with:
  - `buildKdsPayload()` — builds order payload with hashed JID (PDPA), items, table/pickup, timestamp
  - `dispatchToKds()` — POSTs to configurable webhook URL with auth header and 10s timeout
  - In-memory retry queue with exponential backoff (max 3 attempts, configurable)
  - `hashJid()` — SHA-256 first 12 hex chars for privacy-compliant customer identification
  - POS response parsing: accepted/rejected status relayed back to caller
- Extended `CartHandlerOptions` in `src/tools/cart.ts` with `kdsWebhook`, `profileId`, `customerJid`
- Hooked KDS dispatch into `order_confirm_submit` handler — after FnB order creation and order ID extraction:
  - Builds KDS payload from cart items, table info, and session context
  - Dispatches non-blocking — order still succeeds even if KDS is down
  - POS rejection note appended to customer response if POS rejects
- Added `kdsWebhook` config to `src/assistant/data-makan/settings.json` (disabled by default)
- Updated `src/routes/public/webchat-api.ts` to read and pass KDS config from profile settings
- Added 16-test file: `src/assistant/__tests__/kds-webhook.test.ts` covering:
  - JID hashing (consistency, uniqueness, format)
  - Payload building (dine-in, takeaway, walk-in)
  - Dispatch disabled scenarios (enabled=false, empty URL)
  - Successful POST with accepted response
  - Auth header transmission
  - POS rejected response relay
  - HTTP failure with retry queue
  - Network error handling
  - Non-JSON 2xx treated as accepted
  - Payload schema compliance (all acceptance criteria fields)
- Build: esbuild passes (3 pre-existing warnings, 0 errors)
- Tests: 16/16 pass, config-integration test passes

### Patterns discovered
- Cart handlers receive options via `CartHandlerOptions` interface — new features add optional fields
- `webchat-api.ts` `buildMakanMomentsContext()` reads profile settings and passes to `createCartHandlers()`
- Order ID extraction uses regex on FnB MCP response text — `getSessionOrderId()` available after extraction
- KDS dispatch is best placed AFTER FnB order creation (order ID needed) and BEFORE stage transition to PLACED

### Gotchas
- KDS dispatch must be non-blocking — order placement should succeed even if KDS webhook fails
- JID must be hashed for PDPA compliance — never send raw phone numbers to external POS systems
- The `kdsWebhook` config defaults to disabled — zero impact on existing deployments
- Retry queue is in-memory only — server restart loses pending retries (acceptable for MVP, DB fallback noted in story)

## Iteration 5 - Story: US-881 Order modification window after submission

### What was implemented
- New module `src/assistant/order-modification-store.ts`:

  - `setActiveModification()` / `consumeActiveModification()` — one-shot flag for resubmission detection
  - `getModificationRemainingSeconds()` — for UI/prompt context
- `src/tools/cart.ts`:
  - New tool definition `order_modify_request` — restores items to cart within modification window
  - New `CartHandlerOptions.orderModificationWindowMinutes` (default: 2)
  - `order_confirm_submit` now saves snapshot before clearing cart, includes "You have N minutes to make changes" in response
  - Resubmission detection via `consumeActiveModification()` — modified orders say "Your updated order" and skip window message

  - Passes `orderModificationWindowMinutes` from settings to cart handlers

### Patterns discovered
- Linter auto-modifies handler code: snapshot clearing moved before item restore for safety (prevents double-restore)
- One-shot flag pattern (`setActiveModification`/`consumeActiveModification`) cleanly separates modification tracking from snapshot lifecycle
- `setInterval(...).unref()` on cleanup timer to not block process exit

### Gotchas
- Can't rely on snapshot presence for resubmission detection — `order_modify_request` clears snapshot before items are restored (safety measure)
- Kitchen acceptance webhook integration is prepared (`markKitchenAccepted`) but the actual KDS webhook handler needs to call it when POS sends acceptance

- Added setActiveModification/consumeActiveModification flags so order_confirm_submit detects resubmissions and uses "Your updated order" label

- Added 13 new tests covering: modification store unit tests (snapshot save/retrieve, window expiry, kitchen acceptance, remaining seconds, clear, active modification flag) and handler integration tests (restore within window, reject no order, reject expired, reject kitchen accepted, prevent double-restore, resubmission label)

### Patterns discovered
- order-modification-store.ts was already created with full snapshot/timer/kitchen-acceptance logic
- cart.ts already had the tool definition and handler wired up with most logic
- webchat-api.ts already reads orderModificationWindowMinutes from makan settings and passes to createCartHandlers
- The state machine workaround (clearOrderStage after PLACED) means BROWSING → ORDERING transition is used for modification restore

### Gotchas
- The modification snapshot must be cleared BEFORE restoring items, otherwise a second "change order" would add items again (qty doubling)
- Remaining time calculation must happen before clearing the snapshot, since getModificationRemainingSeconds needs the snapshot
- The 0-minute window test needs a small delay (10ms) to ensure the window actually expires

## Iteration - Story: US-882 Proactive abandoned-cart recovery message on WhatsApp session idle
### What was implemented
- New module `src/assistant/cart-idle-recovery.ts`:
  - `buildRecoveryMessage()` — formats cart summary with "Resume order" / "Clear cart" quick-reply options
  - `parseRecoveryReply()` — detects "resume order", "clear cart", "resume", "clear" variants (case-insensitive)
  - `handleRecoveryReply()` — clears cart on "clear", returns welcome-back message on "resume"
  - `checkIdleCarts()` — scans all active cart sessions, sends recovery for idle ones via DB insert (webchat polling)
  - `initCartIdleRecovery()` / `stopCartIdleRecovery()` — periodic checker with configurable idle threshold
  - Auto-clears cart after 24h if recovery message is ignored
- Cart store (`src/assistant/cart-store.ts`) already had US-882 scaffolding:
  - `recoveryMessageSent` / `recoveryMessageSentAt` fields on CartSession
  - `cartMarkRecoverySent()`, `cartIsRecoverySent()`, `cartResetRecovery()`, `cartGetActiveSessions()`
  - `cartAddItem()` auto-resets recovery flag on new cart activity
- `src/routes/public/webchat-api.ts`:
  - Intercepts "Resume order" / "Clear cart" replies before AI processing
  - Resets recovery flag on any user message (user is active again)
  - Persists recovery reply exchange to DB for webchat history
- `src/index.ts`: Initializes cart idle recovery checker after scheduler init, reads `cartIdleRecoveryMinutes` from makan-moments settings
- `src/assistant/data-makan/settings.json`: Added `cartIdleRecoveryMinutes: 30`
- `src/assistant/__tests__/cart-idle-recovery.test.ts`:
  - 15 tests: buildRecoveryMessage (items + options, empty cart), parseRecoveryReply (resume/clear variants, unrelated), handleRecoveryReply (clear/resume/null), cart recovery flags (mark/check/reset/auto-reset on add), checkIdleCarts (sends for idle, no duplicates)

### Patterns discovered
- Cart store already had full recovery flag infrastructure from a previous partial implementation attempt
- The webchat polling pattern (INSERT into rainbow_messages + client polls) works well for proactive messages — no WebSocket needed
- Recovery message is inserted with source='cart-recovery' for easy filtering in analytics

### Gotchas
- Recovery check interval (2 min) must be shorter than the idle threshold (30 min) to catch carts promptly
- Timer uses `.unref()` to not block process exit
- The recovery message interception must happen BEFORE sanitization and AI processing in the webchat handler


## Iteration - Story: US-883 ARIA live region announcements for new webchat widget messages
### What was implemented
- Added `aria-relevant="additions"` to messages container div so screen readers only announce new messages, not re-read entire log
- Added a visually-hidden `<div id="typingStatus" aria-live="polite" role="status">` for typing indicator announcements ("Assistant is typing...")
- Updated `showTyping()` to set typingStatus text, `hideTyping()` to clear it — screen readers announce via the separate live region without interrupting
- Updated `appendMessage()` to postMessage `rainbow-new-message` to parent window for embedded widget badge
- Updated widget.js badge to track `unreadCount` with dynamic `aria-label` ("1 new message" / "N new messages")
- Typing indicator remains `aria-hidden="true"` (visual dots only) — announcement handled by separate status div

### Patterns discovered
- Using a separate visually-hidden live region for status announcements (typing) avoids conflict with the message log's own aria-live
- The `role="log"` + `aria-live="polite"` + `aria-relevant="additions"` combination on the messages container is the recommended WCAG pattern for chat UIs
- Widget badge was already created with static aria-label — just needed dynamic count

### Gotchas
- Must NOT set aria-live on typing indicator div itself — it's inside the messages container which already has aria-live, causing double announcements
- The `animate` parameter distinguishes history restoration (false) from live messages (true) — only live bot messages should trigger parent postMessage


## Iteration - Story: US-882 Proactive abandoned-cart recovery message on WhatsApp session idle
### What was implemented
- Extended `cart-idle-recovery.ts` with WhatsApp outbound support:
  - Added `isWhatsAppSession(sessionId)` to detect phone-number sessions vs webchat UUIDs
  - `checkIdleCarts()` now routes WhatsApp sessions through a configurable send function
  - WhatsApp 24-hour session window compliance: skips sessions idle > 24h
  - `initCartIdleRecovery()` accepts optional `sendWhatsApp` callback to avoid circular deps
- Added WhatsApp pipeline integration in `state-executor.ts`:
  - Early interception of "Resume order" / "Clear cart" replies from WhatsApp users
  - Checks `cartIsRecoverySent(phone)` and `isWhatsAppSession(phone)` before delegating to `handleRecoveryReply`
  - Logs recovery replies and sends response via WhatsApp
- Updated `index.ts` to pass `sendWhatsAppMessage` to `initCartIdleRecovery`
- 21 tests covering: message building, reply parsing, session detection, webchat recovery, WhatsApp recovery (with/without send function), duplicate prevention, cart flags

### Patterns discovered
- Cart sessions use sessionId as key — phone numbers for WhatsApp, UUIDs for webchat
- `isWhatsAppSession` uses regex `/^\d{8,15}$/` to detect phone-number sessions
- WhatsApp send function passed as callback during init to avoid circular dependency between cart-idle-recovery and whatsapp modules
- The `checkIdleCarts` scanner handles both session types in a single loop

### Gotchas
- Without a WhatsApp send function configured, WhatsApp sessions silently skip (with warning log)
- 24h window check uses `idleDuration >= WHATSAPP_24H_WINDOW_MS` — since idle is measured from lastAccess, sessions older than 24h are outside WhatsApp's session window
- Cart tools currently only used from webchat-api.ts — WhatsApp carts require future integration of cart tools into the WhatsApp pipeline



## Iteration - Story: US-847 Admin audit log for configuration changes with before/after diff
### What was implemented
- Schema change: Added `before_data JSONB` and `after_data JSONB` columns to `rainbow_config_audit` table via `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`

### Patterns discovered
- Settings sub-tabs follow a render function pattern: `renderXTab(container)` that injects HTML into `#settings-tab-content`
- The `switchSettingsTab()` function has a guard checking for settings data in cache — tabs with independent data sources must be rendered before that guard

- menu-items.ts and data-retention.ts write directly to `rainbow_config_audit` without before/after — they'll have NULL for those columns which is fine
- The `api()` helper in the frontend prepends `/api/rainbow` to all paths, so audit endpoint is called as `/metrics/config-audit`



## Iteration - Story: US-885 WhatsApp product catalog single-item card for item detail showcase
### What was implemented
- **Product card builders** (`formatter.ts`): Added `ProductCardItem` interface, `buildProductCardText()` (multilingual WhatsApp markdown), `buildProductCardButtons()` (Baileys interactive buttons with "Add to cart" + "View full menu"), `productCardToText()` (plain text fallback with hints)

Fetches rich details via FnB MCP tool for description/allergens/dietary flags.
- **Button press handling** (`state-executor.ts`): Intercepts `add_to_cart:CODE` button presses, looks up item by code or fuzzy match, adds to cart via `cartAddItem`, transitions order stage to ORDERING, sends multilingual confirmation with cart summary. Also handles `view_menu` button by rewriting to "show me the menu" for normal pipeline processing.

### Patterns discovered
- esbuild fails on Chinese fullwidth quotation marks (U+201C/U+201D) inside regular double-quoted strings — must use `\u201C`/`\u201D` Unicode escapes or single-quoted strings
- SPIRAL worker directories (`.spiral-workers/worker-1/`, `worker-2/`) contain modified copies of source files that can mislead reads — always verify actual file on disk
- `fetchMenuItems()` returns `DisambiguationCandidate[]` from the FnB MCP service, compatible with `findMenuItemMatches()` fuzzy matcher

### Gotchas
- Baileys does NOT support WhatsApp Business API "Single Product Message" (catalog integration) — the product card uses interactive buttons message as a fallback
- Button press responses come as `buttonsResponseMessage.selectedDisplayText` (human-readable) or `selectedButtonId` (structured) — state-executor intercepts the structured `add_to_cart:CODE` format
- The `view_menu` button rewrite approach (changing `state.text` to "show me the menu") allows the normal classification pipeline to handle it without special-casing

- **`fnbGetPopularItems()` function** (`tools/fnb-menu.ts`): Calls `fnb_get_popular_items` MCP endpoint with configurable limit (default 5). Falls back to full menu scan, picking items tagged as featured/bestseller. If neither, returns first N items from menu
- **`handlePopularItems()` handler** (`action-dispatch.ts`): Intercepts `food_recommendation` intent in `handleLLMReply`. Calls `fnbGetPopularItems()`, formats multilingual response (EN/MS/ZH) with numbered list, header ("most popular dishes, loved by most guests!"), and footer ("tell me the name or number to add to your order")
- **`formatPopularItemsResponse()` helper**: Numbers items for easy guest selection, adds header and footer in detected language
- **28 tests** covering: response formatting (EN/MS/ZH), single/multi item, empty line handling, language fallback, regex pattern matching for all undecided phrases (EN/MS/ZH), negative cases

### Patterns discovered
- The `food_recommendation` intent was already routed to `llm_reply` — adding a special handler intercept (same pattern as MENU_ITEM_DETAIL, MENU_FILTER_PRICE, MENU_SPECIALS) keeps routing config untouched
- The `fnb_get_popular_items` MCP endpoint may not exist yet on the FnB service — the fallback to menu scan with featured/bestseller tag matching makes this resilient

### Gotchas
- The fallback logic in `fnbGetPopularItems` parses menu text for "featured"/"bestseller" keywords in item names/categories. If no tagged items found, it returns the first N items (reasonable default)
- Guest can pick items by number or name from the recommendation list, which feeds back into existing intent classification (order_placement or MENU_ITEM_DETAIL)

## Iteration 2 - Sun, Mar 15, 2026 - Story: US-926 Handle BSUID in inbound webhooks and route conversations by BSUID
### What was implemented
- **AC1-AC4 were already implemented** from prior work (US-477): extractBsuid in instance.ts, conversationKey/upsertConversation in conversation-db.ts, resolveBsuidToPhone in bsuid-resolver.ts, mergeBsuidConversation for linking existing conversations
- **AC5: Integration test** (`bsuid-routing.test.ts`): Added 16 new tests (28 total) covering:
  - extractBsuid from Baileys message shapes: msg.lid (v7+), msg.key.participant (group/forwarded), resolvedFrom (BSUID-only user), priority ordering
  - resolveBsuidToPhone: passthrough for normal phones/JIDs, null for empty prefix, graceful DB failure for unknown BSUID
  - Full BSUID-only inbound flow simulation: BSUID extraction → conversationKey → outbound resolution
  - Dual-identifier (phone+BSUID): phone wins as primary key, BSUID linked
  - Conversation continuity: pre-existing phone conversation gets BSUID linked, BSUID-only conversation merges when phone becomes available
  - Outbound edge case: bsuid-prefixed DB key resolves to null when no phone mapping exists

### Patterns discovered
- BSUID support was already substantial from US-477 — schema (bsuid column), extraction, conversation keying, outbound resolution, and merge logic all existed
- The pipeline passes `msg.bsuid` throughout all 4 phases (input-validator, state-executor, intent-classifier, response-processor) via spread operator `...(msg.bsuid ? { bsuid: msg.bsuid } : {})`
- For BSUID-only users, outbound delivery uses the raw @lid JID (msg.from), NOT the bsuid-prefixed DB key

### Gotchas
- The in-memory conversation state (conversation.ts) uses raw `msg.from` as key, while DB operations use `conversationKey()` which normalizes to `bsuid:XX.YYY` — this asymmetry is intentional (in-memory is ephemeral)
- resolveBsuidToPhone only triggers for `bsuid:`-prefixed phone strings (admin-initiated outbound), not for raw BSUID JIDs (which pass through unchanged)

## Iteration - Story: US-894 PDPA 2024 Phase 3: machine-readable data portability export with CSV and self-service OTP
### What was implemented
- Added `?format=csv` support to existing `/api/rainbow/guests/:jid/data-export` endpoint
- CSV output is RFC 4180 compliant with UTF-8 BOM for Excel compatibility
- CSV fields: timestamp, direction (inbound/outbound mapped from role), content, intent, sentiment
- Formula-injection sanitization: fields starting with =, +, -, @, \t, \r are prefixed with single quote
- Created self-service public endpoint `/api/data-portability/request` (POST) to send 6-digit OTP via WhatsApp
- Created `/api/data-portability/verify` (POST) to verify OTP and download data in JSON or CSV format
- OTP is cryptographically random, valid 10 minutes, single-use, max 5 attempts
- Timing-safe OTP comparison to prevent side-channel attacks
- Rate limiting: 5 OTP requests and 10 verify attempts per 15-minute window per IP
- Self-service exports logged to gdpr_audit_log with action='portability'
- Refactored data-fetching logic into reusable `fetchGuestData()` and `buildJsonPayload()` functions
- 20 Vitest tests covering: BOM, headers, CRLF, direction mapping, field coverage, quoting, formula injection

### Patterns discovered
- Sentiment is tracked in-memory (StateManager with TTL) but NOT persisted per-message in DB, so CSV sentiment column is empty
- The `role` field maps to direction: user=inbound, assistant=outbound
- Admin routes are all behind adminAuth middleware in routes/admin/index.ts; public routes go in routes/public/ and mount directly on app

### Gotchas
- No sentiment column in rainbow_messages table — sentiment analysis is ephemeral per-session
- Had to export ensureAuditTable from gdpr-data-export.ts for reuse in the public route


## Iteration 3 - Sun, Mar 15, 2026 - Story: US-894 PDPA 2024 Phase 3: machine-readable data portability export with CSV and self-service OTP
### What was implemented
- **Already complete from prior SPIRAL iterations**: All 6 acceptance criteria were already implemented:
  - `src/routes/admin/gdpr-data-export.ts`: JSON+CSV export with BOM, RFC 4180 compliance, formula-injection sanitization, audit logging
  - `src/routes/public/data-portability.ts`: Self-service OTP-verified endpoint (POST /request + POST /verify), rate limiting, timing-safe OTP comparison

- **Verification only**: All 20 tests pass, build succeeds, server starts cleanly

- SPIRAL workers (worker-1, worker-2) had a draft test with incorrect expectations (assumed sanitizeCsvField wraps all fields in double-quotes). The main codebase test was correct.
- The `sanitizeCsvField` prepends a single quote `'` (not tab) to neutralize formula-injection characters (=, +, -, @, \t, \r)
- `escapeCsvField` is the RFC 4180 quoting layer (wraps in double-quotes only when field contains commas, quotes, or newlines)

- The story was already fully implemented but not marked as `passes: true` — verification-only iteration

## Iteration - Story: US-895 Persist raw webhook payloads to durable storage before processing
### What was implemented
- Added `webhook_raw_events` Drizzle table (event_id UUID PK, received_at, source, profile, payload JSONB, processed BOOLEAN) with indexes on received_at, source, processed
- Created `src/lib/webhook-raw-events.ts` module: `persistRawEvent()` (fire-and-forget insert), `markRawEventProcessed()`, `getRawEventById()` — all with their own try/catch to never block the webhook 200 response
- Added `rawEventId` optional field to `IncomingMessage` type in `src/assistant/types.ts`
- Modified ALL webhook routes in `src/routes/webhooks/index.ts` to call `persistRawEvent()` before processing: evolution, digiman, events, meta:quality, meta:account, meta:template-status, meta:capability, kds, meta:messages
- Meta messages route (`/webhooks/meta/messages`) attaches `rawEventId` to each parsed IncomingMessage so it flows through BullMQ to the DLQ
- Updated DLQ entry creation in `src/lib/message-queue.ts` to include `rawEventId` from `job.data.rawEventId`
- Extended `DLQJob` interface with `rawEventId: string | null`
- Added `GET /dlq/:jobId` admin route in `src/routes/admin/dlq.ts` that returns the DLQ job + associated raw event payload via `getRawEventById()`
- Added raw event pruning (30-day retention) to `src/lib/data-retention.ts` runRetentionPurge — deletes webhook_raw_events where received_at < 30 days ago
- Updated `PurgeResult` interface to include `rawEvents` count
- Created 12 Vitest tests in `src/assistant/__tests__/webhook-raw-events.test.ts`: insert success, DB failure resilience, persistence before downstream throw, default/custom profile, markProcessed, getRawEventById (found/not-found/error), IncomingMessage type compatibility

### Patterns discovered
- Fire-and-forget pattern: `persistRawEvent().catch(() => {})` — the `.catch(() => {})` prevents unhandled rejection while ensuring the webhook 200 response is never delayed
- The Meta messages route is the only one that needs `rawEventId` tracking on IncomingMessage because it's the only one that feeds messages into BullMQ via the message handler pipeline
- jsonb import from `drizzle-orm/pg-core` was needed for the payload column

### Gotchas
- Must import `jsonb` separately from drizzle-orm/pg-core — it wasn't in the existing imports
- The DLQ job data structure is `{ originalJobId, data, error, failedAt }` — the `rawEventId` is added as a top-level field alongside these, NOT nested inside `data`

## Iteration 6 - Sun, Mar 15, 2026 - Story: US-927 Prompt injection input sanitisation layer in message processing pipeline
### What was implemented
- **Enhanced `prompt-injection-guard.ts`**: Added 7 OWASP LLM01 regex patterns (instruction_override, role_hijack, prompt_extraction, delimiter_injection, encoded_injection, multi_turn_manipulation, jailbreak_attempt) on top of existing substring matching
- **Output validation**: Added `detectSystemPromptLeakage()` function — sliding-window check (80-char chunks) to detect if LLM response contains system prompt fragments, plus "my instructions are" self-disclosure pattern detection
- **DB logging**: Added `prompt_injection_log` table (id, jid, profile_id, raw_message, matched_pattern, action, created_at) with indexes on created_at and jid
- **Logging module** (`src/lib/prompt-injection-log.ts`): Fire-and-forget `logPromptInjection()` + `getInjectionAttemptStats()` for admin dashboard (count, daily trend, recent 20 attempts)
- **Pipeline wiring**: Input validator now calls `logPromptInjection()` when blocking injection. Response processor calls `detectSystemPromptLeakage()` and replaces leaked responses with profile fallback
- **Admin API**: `GET /injection-attempts` endpoint returning totalCount, dailyTrend (configurable days, default 7), and recentAttempts
- **46 Vitest tests**: 12 injection phrase tests, case insensitivity, 20 safe messages (hostel + cafe), custom patterns, 5 OWASP regex tests, 6 system prompt leakage tests

### Patterns discovered
- Regex patterns complement substring matching — substrings catch exact phrases while regex catches structural patterns (delimiters, encoding attempts)
- System prompt leakage detection uses 80-char sliding window with 20-char stride — balances detection sensitivity vs false positives
- Fire-and-forget DB logging pattern (same as escalation events) prevents injection logging from blocking the message response

### Gotchas
- Regex patterns only run when using default patterns (not custom), to avoid double-matching when users configure their own patterns
- The "act as" substring pattern is very broad — could match legitimate messages like "act as soon as possible". Kept for backwards compatibility but OWASP regex provides more targeted detection
- System prompt leakage check requires prompt >= 80 chars to use sliding window; short prompts rely solely on self-disclosure pattern matching
## Iteration 1 - Sun, Mar 15, 2026  6:02:00 PM
Completed: Prompt injection input sanitisation layer in message processing pipeline (ID: US-927) in 12m


## Iteration - Story: US-927 Prompt injection input sanitisation layer in message processing pipeline
### What was implemented
- **All core infrastructure was already in place** from prior iterations:
  - `src/assistant/pipeline/prompt-injection-guard.ts`: Multi-layer detection — Layer 1a substring matching (30 patterns), Layer 1b OWASP LLM01 regex patterns (7 categories: instruction_override, role_hijack, prompt_extraction, delimiter_injection, encoded_injection, multi_turn_manipulation, jailbreak_attempt), Layer 2 output leakage detection via `detectSystemPromptLeakage()`
  - `src/assistant/pipeline/input-validator.ts`: Calls `detectPromptInjection()` + `logPromptInjection()` on every inbound message before LLM
  - `src/assistant/pipeline/response-processor.ts`: Calls `detectSystemPromptLeakage()` on every LLM response, replaces with fallback if system prompt leakage detected
  - `src/lib/prompt-injection-log.ts`: Fire-and-forget DB logging with `logPromptInjection()`, `getInjectionAttemptStats()` for analytics
  - `shared/schema-tables.ts`: `promptInjectionLog` table with jid, profile_id, raw_message, matched_pattern, action, created_at
  - `src/routes/admin/injection-attempts.ts`: GET /injection-attempts admin endpoint with count, 7-day trend, recent attempts
  - `src/routes/admin/index.ts`: Route registered as `router.use(injectionAttemptsRoutes)`
- **Added missing tests** to `src/assistant/__tests__/prompt-injection-guard.test.ts`:
  - 5 tests for OWASP LLM01 regex patterns (instruction_override, delimiter_injection, encoded_injection, jailbreak, prompt_extraction)
  - 6 tests for `detectSystemPromptLeakage` (verbatim, chunk, normal response, self-disclosure, empty, short prompt)
  - 1 test for AC4 false-positive rate: 50-message safe corpus verifies 0% FP (< 1% threshold)
  - Total: 47 tests, all passing

### Patterns discovered
- Substring matching runs before regex — test inputs for regex-specific tests must NOT contain any DEFAULT_PATTERNS substrings
- The 80-char sliding window in `detectSystemPromptLeakage` only activates for system prompts ≥ 80 chars (short prompts like "Be helpful." won't trigger it)
- `logPromptInjection()` is fire-and-forget — it doesn't block the 200 response

### Gotchas
- test for "delimiter injection" initially failed because "You are now" in the test message triggered substring matching first — fixed by using "Please comply" instead
- test for "prompt extraction" initially failed because "system prompt" in the test message triggered substring matching — fixed by using "your persona configuration" (matches regex but no DEFAULT_PATTERN substring)

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-896 Webchat widget dark mode support via CSS prefers-color-scheme
### What was implemented
- **FOUC prevention**: Inline `<script>` in `<head>` before `<style>` reads `theme` query param and applies `.dark-theme` or `.light-theme` class to `<html>` before first paint
- **Dark mode CSS variables**: `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` block with `:root:not(.light-theme)` selector overrides all palette tokens (bg, surface, text, text-muted, border, user/bot bubble colors)
- **Forced dark mode**: `.dark-theme` class applies same dark palette tokens regardless of OS preference
- **Desktop dark adjustments**: Darker outer background (#020617), stronger box shadow for chat container in dark mode
- **Component adjustments**: Bot bubble shadow increased opacity, textarea gets explicit bg/color in dark mode, consent overlay darkened
- **Widget embed script**: Added `data-theme` / `theme` config parameter ('light'|'dark'|'auto'), passes to iframe URL as query param
- **Playwright e2e test**: 4 tests covering OS-dark, OS-light, forced-dark override, forced-light override with screenshot capture
### Patterns discovered
- Spiral worker had already implemented this story — Ralph validated and applied changes to main source
- CSS custom properties (variables) in `:root` make dark mode trivial — just override the tokens
- `:root:not(.light-theme)` inside `@media prefers-color-scheme: dark` allows forced light override
- FOUC prevention requires inline script BEFORE stylesheet in `<head>`
### Status
Build passes. Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration 4 - Story: US-908 Enforce tenant_id isolation at database layer
### What was implemented
- `shared/schema-tables.ts`: Added `tenantId` to `adminUsers` (RBAC scoping), `appSettings` (tenant-scoped unique key), `intentDetectionSettings`; changed `app_settings.key` unique to composite `(tenantId, key)`; made `profileId` non-nullable in `rainbowMessages` and `rainbowConversations`

- `src/lib/session-window.ts`: Added optional `tenantId` param to `sessionWindowActive` and `batchSessionWindowStatus` — adds `AND profile_id = $n` clause when provided
- `src/assistant/escalation.ts`: Added `tenantId` param to `fetchLastDbMessages` raw pg query, passes `profileId` from context to prevent cross-property message leakage in handoff summaries
- `src/assistant/conversation-logger-types.ts`: Added `tenantId?: string` to `ConversationLog` and `ConversationSummary`
- `src/lib/message-scheduler.ts`: Added `profileId?: string` to `ScheduledMessage` interface
- `src/lib/tenant-context.ts` (NEW): Tenant isolation middleware — reads resolved `profileId`, validates against `KNOWN_TENANTS`, enforces admin RBAC scoping (403 if operator tries cross-tenant access), sets `res.locals.tenantId`
- `src/routes/admin/index.ts`: Mounted `tenantContextMiddleware` after profile resolution middleware
- `src/assistant/__tests__/tenant-isolation.test.ts` (NEW): 14 unit tests covering middleware, RBAC scoping, super-admin bypass, and backward compat
### Patterns discovered
- `profileId` is the existing tenant identifier; we layer `tenantId` as an enforced alias via middleware
- Raw pg queries in escalation.ts and session-window.ts lacked `profile_id` filter — critical cross-property data leakage risk
- `app_settings.key` was globally unique — changed to composite `(tenantId, key)` unique index
- `rainbow_kb_files` is a raw pg table (not Drizzle) so its migration lives in config-db.ts
- `super-admin` role bypasses tenant scoping; other roles are restricted to `adminTenantId`
### Gotchas
- `adminTenantId` must be populated in res.locals from the admin JWT/session; current auth only sets `adminRole` — future story should populate `adminTenantId` from the `adminUsers.tenantId` DB column on login
- `rainbow_configs` table stores JSON configs globally — not yet tenant-scoped (future story)
### Status
✅ Build passes. 14/14 tenant-isolation tests pass. Marked passes=true.

## Iteration N - Story: US-927 WhatsApp Flows Data API v4.0 Two-Signature Auth
### What was implemented
- Updated `src/assistant/data/flows/reservation-flow.json`: data_api_version 3.0 → 4.0
- Added `verifyFlowPlatformSignature()` and `verifyFlowTokenSignature()` to `src/lib/whatsapp/flow-crypto.ts`
  - Platform sig: HMAC-SHA256 of raw body using META_APP_SECRET (X-Hub-Signature-256 header)
  - Token sig: optional HMAC-SHA256 of flow_token using RAINBOW_FLOWS_TOKEN_SECRET (X-Hub-Flow-Token-Signature header)
- Updated `src/routes/public/whatsapp-flows.ts`: signature verification before decryption
  - Platform sig checked on every request when META_APP_SECRET is set
  - Token sig checked when RAINBOW_FLOWS_VERIFY_TOKEN_SIG=true
  - Rejected signatures return HTTP 401 with security event logged (reason + timestamp)
- Created `src/routes/public/__tests__/whatsapp-flows-v4.test.ts`: 9 unit tests (all passing)

### Patterns discovered
- rawBody is available on all requests via captureRawBody middleware wired into express.json()
- META_APP_SECRET is already used for webhook validation — same secret reused for Flows v4.0
- Flows endpoint type must be `Request & { rawBody?: Buffer }` to access raw body

### Gotchas
- The warning about META_APP_SECRET fires at module load time (not per-request) to match webhook pattern
- prd.json has Unicode content requiring explicit UTF-8 encoding in Python
