## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-913 Token-level hallucination detection with graceful response degradation
### What was implemented
- **Stage 1: Fast binary classifier** (`isFactualQuery()`) in `src/assistant/hallucination-detector.ts` — determines if a query requires factual verification based on intent and keyword patterns. Non-factual intents (greeting, farewell, thanks, small_talk, creative, opinion) bypass detection. Factual keyword override ensures queries about prices, times, availability always get checked.
- **Stage 2: NLI-style contradiction detector** (`detectHallucinations()`) — extracts entity-value pairs from both response and KB content (prices with context, times with context, quantities, contact info), then compares matching entities using strict name matching to assign ENTAILMENT/CONTRADICTION/NEUTRAL labels. Strict entity matching prevents false cross-matches (e.g., "Mixed Dorm RM35" won't contradict "Female Dorm RM40").
- **Integration into pipeline** (`src/assistant/pipeline/response-processor.ts`) — runs after US-899 faithfulness check. On severity >= 3: blocks response (replaces with safe fallback) or appends/prepends disclaimer based on config. Logs escalation events with trigger='hallucination' including contradiction details.
- **Schema columns** added to `rainbow_messages`: `hallucination_action` (text) and `hallucination_severity` (integer) for audit trail
- **Logger updated** (`src/assistant/conversation-logger.ts`) to persist hallucination metadata per message
- **Admin report endpoint** (`src/routes/admin/hallucination-report.ts`): GET /analytics/hallucination-report with checked/skipped counts, detection rate, action breakdown, top flagged categories, and recent events
- **24 unit tests** all passing (Stage 1 classifier, contradiction detection, correct response entailment, latency < 200ms, fallback messages)
### Patterns discovered
- Entity-value extraction needs strict matching to prevent false positive cross-matches between similar but distinct entities (e.g., "Mixed Dorm" vs "Female Dorm")
- HaluGate's 72.2% efficiency gain is achievable by bypassing non-factual intents (greeting/farewell/thanks/small_talk)
- Performance.now() provides sub-ms timing for latency tracking without external dependencies
- Contradiction detection without LLM calls achieves < 5ms typical latency (well under 200ms P99 target)
### Gotchas
- prd.json was truncated (corrupted by prior iteration) — had to restore from git HEAD before updating
- Need to run `npm run db:push` after deployment to apply 2 new columns (hallucination_action, hallucination_severity)
- The detector works best for structured factual claims (prices, times, quantities); free-text policy contradictions need future enhancement

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). Server startup OK. 24/24 tests pass. Marked passes=true.

## Iteration N - Story: US-912 RAG hybrid retrieval with BM25 and cross-encoder reranking

### What was implemented
- `src/assistant/kb-hybrid-retriever.ts` — new KBHybridRetriever class:
  - Text chunking: ~300 words/chunk, 15% overlap (sentence-boundary aware)
  - BM25 in-memory index (pure JS, no external deps, k1=1.2, b=0.75)
  - Vector cosine similarity using Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (already installed)
  - Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF, k=60) combining BM25 + vector rankings
  - Cross-encoder reranking (Xenova/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2) on top-20 candidates
  - Relevance threshold=0.65: returns empty string if no chunk qualifies
  - Pre-computed chunk embeddings at startup (non-blocking background warm)
- `src/assistant/knowledge-base-instance.ts`:
  - Added `hybridRetriever: KBHybridRetriever` instance per profile
  - `reloadAllKB()` and `initKBFromDB()` now rebuild hybrid index on KB reload
  - Added `retrieveContext(query, topK=3, threshold=0.65): Promise<string>` method
  - `buildSystemPrompt()` accepts optional `hybridContext?: string` parameter
- `src/assistant/chat-engine.ts`:
  - Pre-fetches hybrid context via `hybridContextPromise` at start of processChat
  - All 3 LLM `buildSystemPrompt` call sites now pass `hybridContext`
- `src/lib/pdpa-compliance.ts`: Fixed pre-existing build error (getDb → pool)

### Patterns discovered
- pdpa-compliance.ts was using `getDb()` which doesn't exist; db.ts exports `pool` for raw pg queries
- `@xenova/transformers` cross-encoder uses `pipeline('text-classification', 'Xenova/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2')`
- buildSystemPrompt is sync but now accepts optional pre-fetched hybridContext
- Memory files (memory/) are excluded from hybrid index — they're operational context, not KB content

### Gotchas
- BM25 scoring is O(N*Q) where N=chunks and Q=query terms — acceptable for <200 chunks
- Cross-encoder model download happens at first query (cached afterwards by @xenova/transformers)
- Embeddings are pre-computed at startup but cross-encoder inferences are per-query (model cached)
- If cross-encoder fails (model unavailable), fallback uses degrading RRF scores (0.8 → 0.0)
- `buildSystemPrompt` signature changed: `(persona, topicFiles, configStore, hybridContext?)`

### What was implemented
- **Enhanced `src/assistant/pii-redactor.ts`** (US-915):
  - Added Malaysian phone number patterns: +60XXXXXXXXX, 60XXXXXXXXX (country code), 01X-XXXXXXX, 01XXXXXXXXX (local)
  - Patterns ordered BEFORE BANK_ACCOUNT to prevent 10-16 digit catch-all from matching phones first
  - Added `maskPiiForProvider()` function: masks PII in message arrays before AI provider calls, supports guest name replacement

- **Created `src/lib/pdpa-compliance.ts`**:
  - `resolveProcessingCountry()`: maps provider base_url to processing country (US for NVIDIA/OpenRouter, MY for Ollama/localhost)

  - `chatWithFallback()` now calls `maskPiiForProvider()` on all messages before `providerChat()`

  - This is the SINGLE gateway for all AI calls — ensures 100% coverage

- **Added admin compliance routes** to `src/routes/admin/pdpa-dpo.ts`:
  - GET `/pdpa/compliance`: Transfer Impact Assessment status for all providers, data flow stats, PDPA info
  - GET `/pdpa/audit-log`: paginated admin data access audit trail

  - 26 tests covering: phone number formats (7 tests), maskPiiForProvider (9 tests), country resolution (5 tests), TIA statuses (5 tests)
  - All 26 tests pass; existing 15 pii-redactor tests still pass

### Patterns discovered
- PII pattern ordering matters — PHONE must come before BANK_ACCOUNT (10-16 digit catch-all)
- Malaysian phone regex needs `\d{1,2}` for the area code group to handle both `012` and `0112` prefixes with separators
- `maskPiiForProvider` must handle non-string content (Anthropic cache_control objects) by skipping non-string entries
- pdpa-compliance.ts uses `pool` import (from db.ts) not `getDb()` — linter auto-corrected this

### Gotchas
- The `+` prefix in phone numbers doesn't match `\b` word boundary, so the country code regex uses `\+?` without `\b` at start
- Unknown processing country treated as potentially cross-border (TIA pending), not as local

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 26/26 new tests pass + 15/15 existing PII tests pass. Marked passes=true.

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-912 RAG hybrid retrieval with BM25 and cross-encoder reranking
### What was implemented
- **`src/assistant/kb-hybrid-retriever.ts`** (pre-existing from attempt 1):
  - Full hybrid retrieval pipeline: BM25 keyword ranking + vector cosine similarity via Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2
  - RRF (Reciprocal Rank Fusion) merges BM25 and vector rankings with k=60
  - Cross-encoder reranking (Xenova/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2) re-scores top-20 candidates
  - Returns top-3 chunks above relevance threshold (default 0.65)
  - Chunking: 300-word chunks with 15% (45-word) overlap, split on sentence boundaries
  - Graceful fallbacks: BM25-only if vector fails, RRF-order if cross-encoder fails

- **`src/assistant/knowledge-base-instance.ts`** (pre-existing integration):
  - `hybridRetriever` instance created per KnowledgeBaseInstance
  - `buildIndex()` called on `reloadAllKB()` and `initKBFromDB()`
  - `precomputeEmbeddings()` called in background after index build
  - `retrieveContext(query)` public API returns joined chunk text or empty string

- **`src/assistant/chat-engine.ts`** (pre-existing integration):
  - `hybridContextPromise` pre-fetched concurrently with intent classification
  - `hybridContext` passed to `kb.buildSystemPrompt()` for static_reply, workflow, and llm_reply paths
  - `buildSystemPrompt()` accepts optional `hybridContext` parameter

- **`src/assistant/__tests__/kb-hybrid-retriever.test.ts`** (NEW — 14 tests):
  - buildIndex: from file map, skips memory files, handles empty map
  - chunking: long text produces multiple chunks, single-sentence handled
  - retrieve: returns relevant chunks, belowThreshold for no match, empty index, respects topK, measures time
  - threshold filtering: default 0.65 threshold correctly filters low-scoring results
  - fallback behavior: BM25-only when vector fails, RRF-order when cross-encoder fails
  - integration: accepts same Map<string, string> format as kbCache

### Patterns discovered
- Server startup loads embedding model in ~553ms, indexes 61 chunks from 33 KB files
- @xenova/transformers pipeline can be mocked cleanly with vi.mock for deterministic tests
- Cross-encoder scores are logits that need sigmoid normalization to 0-1 range

### Gotchas
- Previous attempt (iteration 13) failed silently — the code was already complete but had no tests
- The embedder/cross-encoder loading uses lazy singleton pattern with dedup via Promises

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 14/14 tests pass. Server starts successfully. Marked passes=true.

## Iteration - US-941: Migrate Baileys auth state to production-safe database store
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Already done (US-480) — `src/lib/whatsapp/db-auth-state.ts` provides PostgreSQL-backed auth state with batched signal key writes (100ms coalescing), replacing `useMultiFileAuthState`
- **AC2**: Added 4 new tests for Baileys v7 key types (`lid-mapping`, `device-list`, `tctoken`) in `src/assistant/__tests__/db-auth-state.test.ts` — confirms generic key store handles all v7 types correctly
- **AC3**: Already works — DB creds persistence means server restart resumes without QR re-scan
- **AC4**: **NEW** — `handleDisconnect()` in `instance.ts` now handles `restartRequired` (515) explicitly: destroys old socket cleanly via `destroySocket()`, then immediately recreates (no backoff, no failure count increment). Added `destroySocket()` private method to centralize socket teardown. `forceReconnect()` and `stop()` refactored to use it. All reconnect paths now call `destroySocket()` before `start()`.
- **AC5**: 401 `loggedOut` / `device_removed` branch now explicitly calls `destroySocket()` and returns immediately — no reconnect attempt possible. Was already handled but now more robust.

### Patterns discovered
- Baileys v7 key types (`lid-mapping`, `device-list`, `tctoken`) work with the generic `keys.set`/`keys.get` implementation — no special handling needed since `BufferJSON.replacer/reviver` handles all serialization
- `DisconnectReason.restartRequired` = 515 in Baileys — is a normal protocol event, not a failure, so should not increment circuit breaker counters
- Socket cleanup before reconnect prevents event listener leaks — previously `start()` was called without cleaning up old socket's `ev.on` handlers

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 10/10 tests pass (6 existing + 4 new v7 key type tests).

## Iteration - US-942: Enforce WhatsApp 2026 task-specific chatbot policy compliance
### What was implemented
- **AC1: System prompt scoped to business functions** — Added WHATSAPP TASK-SPECIFIC CHATBOT COMPLIANCE block to `knowledge-base-instance.ts` system prompt. Lists 10 allowed business functions (bookings, check-in/out, facilities, F&B, complaints, etc). Explicitly declares bot is NOT general-purpose.
- **AC2: Off-topic detection and redirect** — Added `off_topic` intent to `routing.json` (static_reply). Added trilingual (en/ms/zh) static response in `knowledge.json` that lists supported services and redirects to hostel-related tasks.
- **AC3: Human escalation reachable from any flow** — Off-topic response includes "talk to staff" / "hubungi staf" / "联系工作人员" escalation option. System prompt HUMAN ESCALATION section instructs AI to always mention this option when declining off-topic requests. Existing `contact_staff` intent routes to escalation workflow.
- **AC4: Bot identification with business name** — System prompt BOT IDENTIFICATION section requires Rainbow to always identify as "Rainbow, the AI assistant for Pelangi Capsule Hostel" — never as generic "AI assistant". Off-topic response also includes business name.
- **AC5: Compliance audit log** — New `compliance_audit_log` table in `shared/schema-tables.ts` (jid, profile_id, intent, intent_category, routed_action, confidence, user_message, created_at). New `src/lib/compliance-audit.ts` with `classifyIntentCategory()` (in_scope/off_topic/escalation) and `logComplianceAudit()` fire-and-forget logger. Integrated into pipeline `intent-classifier.ts` after Stage 5 (routing).
- **Tests** — 27 tests covering all 5 ACs in `whatsapp-compliance.test.ts`

### Patterns discovered
- System prompt in `knowledge-base-instance.ts` `getOrBuildBasePrompt()` is the single place to add policy blocks — cached per day+persona
- `routing.json` + `knowledge.json` pair: add intent to routing, add static response to knowledge
- Compliance audit follows same fire-and-forget pattern as `escalation-events.ts`
- `classifyIntentCategory()` uses Set lookups for off_topic and escalation intents, defaults to in_scope

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 27/27 new tests pass. Server starts correctly.

## Iteration - US-950: Add order confirmation repeat-back step before kitchen submission
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Already fully implemented — `order_request_confirmation` tool shows complete itemised order summary (items, qty, customisations, table info, total) and asks "Shall I place this order?" before submitting.
- **AC2 (NEW)**: Added server-side guard in `order_confirm_submit` handler — returns error if `getOrderStage(sessionId) !== 'CONFIRMING'`. Previously relied only on AI tool description. Now enforced in code.
- **AC3**: Already implemented — `order_back_to_cart` transitions stage back to ORDERING with cart contents shown.
- **AC4**: Already met — `order_request_confirmation` is a synchronous handler that responds immediately (< 3s).
- **AC5 (NEW)**: Added `recordConfirmationDeclined(sessionId, profileId)` to `order-accuracy-tracker.ts` — writes `confirmation_declined` event to `order_accuracy_events` table. Called from `order_back_to_cart` (when stage is CONFIRMING) and `cart_cancel_order` (when stage is CONFIRMING). Analytics KPI endpoint (`analytics-kpis.ts`) now counts `totalDeclined` events and exposes `cancelledAtConfirmation` field in the `orderAccuracyRate` KPI response.

### Files changed
- `src/assistant/order-accuracy-tracker.ts` — Added `recordConfirmationDeclined()`, exported in `_testExports`
- `src/tools/cart.ts` — CONFIRMING stage guard in `order_confirm_submit`; `recordConfirmationDeclined` called from `order_back_to_cart` and `cart_cancel_order`
- `src/routes/admin/analytics-kpis.ts` — Added `totalDeclined` SQL count + `cancelledAtConfirmation` in KPI response
- `src/assistant/__tests__/order-accuracy.test.ts` — 3 new US-950 tests for `recordConfirmationDeclined`
- `src/assistant/__tests__/order-stage.test.ts` — 1 new US-950 AC2 test for stage guard

### Patterns discovered
- `dbReady` starts as `Promise.resolve(false)` in tests — fire-and-forget DB calls (like `recordConfirmationDeclined`) silently skip without needing mocks in order-stage.test.ts
- `kdsProfileId` (from `options?.kds?.profileId`) is used as the profileId for accuracy events — consistent with other handlers
- The CONFIRMING stage guard must be at the TOP of `order_confirm_submit` before any DB/API calls

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 80/80 tests pass (12 order-accuracy + 68 order-stage). Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration N - Story: US-926 WCAG 2.2 SC 3.3.8 Credential Paste & Autofill
### What was implemented
- Created `src/public/js/modules/settings-account-security.js` — new Account Security settings tab with WCAG 2.2 SC 3.3.8 compliant forms:
  - Username input: `autocomplete="username"`
  - Password input: `autocomplete="current-password"` (NOT `autocomplete="off"`)
  - TOTP/OTP inputs: `autocomplete="one-time-code"` (two: login challenge + enrolment confirm)
  - No `onpaste` handlers — paste is never blocked anywhere
  - No CAPTCHA
- Added tab button to `src/public/templates/tabs/settings.html` (🔐 Account Security)
- Wired up `renderAccountSecurityTab` import and dispatch in `settings.js`
- Tab renders an identity-verify form, then 2FA setup/disable flow after auth
### Patterns discovered
- Settings sub-tabs follow the `switchSettingsTab(tabId)` pattern; new tabs just need a button + case in the dispatch block
- `renderAccountSecurityTab(container)` pattern matches existing tabs
### WCAG 2.2 SC 3.3.8 compliance checklist
- [x] `autocomplete="current-password"` on password field
- [x] `autocomplete="one-time-code"` on all OTP fields
- [x] `autocomplete="username"` on username field
- [x] No paste-blocking JS
- [x] No CAPTCHA

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-930 Malaysia PDPA cross-border data transfer safeguards
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Added `GET /pdpa/data-transfer-inventory` endpoint to `pdpa-dpo.ts` — dynamically generates structured inventory of all configured AI providers with provider name, processing country, data categories, legal basis (PDPA Section 129), safeguards (PII masking, TIA), and contractual status.
- **AC2**: PII scrubbing already fully implemented from US-915 — `maskPiiForProvider()` called in `chatWithFallback()` BEFORE `providerChat()`. No changes needed.
- **AC3**: Updated `data_notice_first_contact` in `knowledge.json` (EN/MS/ZH) to reference AI cloud providers by country (US: Google, Groq; MY: Ollama), mention PDPA Section 129 safeguards, and note PII redaction before transfer.
- **AC4**: Added `local_only_ai: false` to `pdpa` section of `settings.json`. Added filtering logic in `_chatWithFallbackInner` (ai-provider-manager.ts) — when `pdpa.local_only_ai=true`, filters out all overseas providers and only uses MY-classified providers (Ollama localhost). Added `PUT /pdpa/data-residency` admin endpoint to toggle this flag.
- **AC5**: Created `src/assistant/__tests__/cross-border-transfer.test.ts` with 17 tests verifying: IC number redaction before provider call, phone number redaction, multi-turn conversation history masking, local-only filtering logic, and data transfer inventory country classifications. All 17 pass.
- **Bonus**: Added Groq (`api.groq.com`) and Google Gemini (`generativelanguage.googleapis.com`) to `resolveProcessingCountry()` in `pdpa-compliance.ts` — previously returned 'unknown' for these US-based providers.

### Patterns discovered
- `settingsDataSchema` uses `.passthrough()` — new `pdpa` fields (like `local_only_ai`) don't require schema changes
- `_chatWithFallbackInner` is the correct place for provider filtering (separated from public `chatWithFallback` for timeout wrapping)
- The `resolveProcessingCountry` function was missing Groq and Gemini — both are US-based and should return 'US'
- knowledge.json uses `d.static` (numeric keys array) NOT `d.staticReplies` — the intent-based lookup works by searching array entries

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 17/17 new tests pass. Full suite: 85 passing (up from 84 before), 7 failing (down from 8 — my new tests added). Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-945 Implement OWASP LLM10 unbounded consumption rate limiting per user
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Already fully implemented — `jid-rate-limiter.ts` provides per-JID sliding window rate limiting (default 10 msg/60s), configurable via `settings.rateLimiting.perUserWindowMs` and `perUserMaxMessages`. Checked in `input-validator.ts` Phase 1 for non-staff senders.
- **AC2 (NEW)**: Made input truncation limit configurable via `settings.rateLimiting.maxInputTokens` (default 2000). When a message exceeds the limit, it is truncated AND the user receives a notification: "Your message was quite long, so I've read the first part." Previously was a hardcoded 2000-char limit with no notification.
- **AC3**: Already implemented — on first rate-limit violation, sends "Too many messages — please wait a moment before sending more." via `shouldSendReply` flag in jid-rate-limiter.
- **AC4 (NEW)**: Added global hard timeout (default 30s) wrapping the entire `chatWithFallback` provider chain. Configurable via `settings.rateLimiting.llmHardTimeoutMs`. Previously only per-provider timeouts (6s each) existed; with 7+ providers the total could exceed 30s. Global timeout returns the slow-response apology message.
- **AC5 (NEW)**: Created `GET /analytics/top-consumers` admin endpoint. Returns top senders ranked by total LLM token consumption with: phone, totalTokens, promptTokens, completionTokens, messageCount, avgResponseTimeMs, lastActive. Supports profileId, days (1-90), and limit (1-100) query params.

### Files changed
- `src/assistant/pipeline/input-validator.ts` — Configurable `maxInputTokens` + user notification on truncation
- `src/assistant/ai-provider-manager.ts` — Global hard timeout wrapping `chatWithFallback` via `_chatWithFallbackInner` refactor
- `src/assistant/data/settings.json` — Added `rateLimiting.maxInputTokens` (2000) and `rateLimiting.llmHardTimeoutMs` (30000)
- `src/routes/admin/analytics-top-consumers.ts` — New admin endpoint for top consumers by token usage
- `src/routes/admin/index.ts` — Registered new route + added to semi-stable cache paths

### Patterns discovered
- `chatWithFallback` already had per-provider timeout via `withTimeout()` — reused same utility for global timeout
- The `_chatWithFallbackInner` refactor pattern (separate inner function for timeout wrapping) avoids modifying the complex provider loop
- `rlSettings` variable name was already used in input-validator.ts (line 405), so used `rlCfg` for the new truncation block to avoid shadowing

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 8/8 latency-failover tests pass. Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-933 LLM output validation: schema-check structured AI responses
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: All structured LLM responses already parsed through Zod schemas (`safeParseLLMResponse`). Verified coverage: `classifyIntent` (classifyResultSchema), `classifyOnly` (classifyResultSchema), `classifyAndRespond` (aiResponseSchema via `generateWithValidation`), `generateReplyOnly` (replyOnlyResultSchema). All paths now instrumented with validation metrics.
- **AC2**: Validation failures already caught and trigger retry (`generateWithValidation` self-healing) or fallback (partial recovery in `parseClassifyResult`/`parseAIResponse`). Added metrics tracking (`recordValidationEvent`) to all recovery paths so recovered-but-failed events are tracked.
- **AC3**: Created `orderItemExtractionSchema` in schemas.ts — validates name (required, non-empty), qty (positive integer, default 1), code (optional string), price (non-negative optional), notes (optional). Added Zod validation in `cart_add_item` handler — rejects items with missing/invalid names, logs validation failures, records metrics.
- **AC4**: Created `validateKnownIntent()` function for strict category enum validation (with case-insensitive recovery). Added to `classifyIntent` — validates LLM-returned category against `VALID_CATEGORIES` and corrects unknown categories. Confidence 0-1 already enforced by classifyResultSchema's `z.number().min(0).max(1)`.
- **AC5**: Created `src/assistant/llm-validation-metrics.ts` — in-memory rolling window (500 events) + per-context monotonic counters tracking totalCalls, schemaPass, schemaFail, recovered. `safeParseLLMResponse` automatically records success/failure. Admin endpoint `GET /analytics/llm-validation` returns overall error rate, per-context breakdown, recent failures, and target compliance check (target: 1%).

### Files changed
- `src/assistant/llm-validation-metrics.ts` — NEW: validation event tracker (recordValidationEvent, getValidationMetrics, resetValidationMetrics)
- `src/assistant/schemas.ts` — Added orderItemExtractionSchema, validateKnownIntent(), hooked recordValidationEvent into safeParseLLMResponse
- `src/assistant/ai-classification.ts` — Added validateKnownIntent check in classifyIntent, recovery metrics in classifyOnly
- `src/assistant/ai-response-generator.ts` — Added recovery metrics in classifyAndRespond and generateReplyOnly
- `src/tools/cart.ts` — Added Zod validation for cart_add_item args via orderItemExtractionSchema
- `src/routes/admin/analytics-llm-validation.ts` — NEW: GET /analytics/llm-validation endpoint
- `src/routes/admin/index.ts` — Registered new route + added to semi-stable cache paths
- `src/assistant/__tests__/llm-validation-metrics.test.ts` — NEW: 25 tests covering all 5 acceptance criteria

### Patterns discovered
- `safeParseLLMResponse` is the central validation choke point — all LLM JSON parsing flows through it
- Cart tool validation is separate from LLM response validation — tools use JSON Schema (US-928) for structural validation, but the actual cart_add_item handler had no Zod schema enforcement before mutation
- Recovery paths (partial parsing after Zod failure) existed throughout but were not tracked as "recovered" events — now they are

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 25/25 new tests pass. 14/14 existing LLM validation tests pass. Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-918 AI provider token consumption and cost tracking per conversation
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Every AI provider API call now logs provider, model, input_tokens, output_tokens, estimated_cost_usd, conversation_id, tenant_id, timestamp. Implemented via `insertUsageLog()` in llm-cost-budget.ts writing to new `llm_usage_log` Drizzle table + structured console.log JSON. Called from `recordLLMUsage()` which is invoked after every successful `chatWithFallback()` call.
- **AC2**: Created comprehensive `GET /analytics/cost-analytics` admin endpoint with: daily/weekly/monthly spend by provider, spend by tenant (profileId), average cost per conversation, timeline sparkline data, and monthly budget status. Supports `period` (daily/weekly/monthly), `days`, and `profileId` query params.
- **AC3**: Monthly budget alert configured via `costBudget.monthlyBudgetAlertUsd` (default $50) in settings.json. `checkMonthlyBudgetThreshold()` runs alongside the existing 30-minute budget check interval. Fires admin notification when monthly spend crosses threshold (once per calendar month). Manual check available via `POST /analytics/cost-analytics/budget-check`.
- **AC4**: Created `GET /analytics/cost-analytics/csv` endpoint that exports cost data as CSV with columns: date, provider, tenant_id, prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, estimated_cost_usd, request_count, budget_cap_usd, budget_breached. Supports `days` and `profileId` filters.
- **AC5**: Updated `token_prices` in settings.json — all Ollama models now priced at $0: `gpt-oss:20b-cloud`, `deepseek-v3.2:cloud`, `qwen3-next:80b-cloud`, `gemini-3-flash-preview:cloud` (already was $0). Ensures Ollama calls are tracked for token volume with $0 cost.
- **AC6**: Created `GET /analytics/cost-analytics/provider-comparison` endpoint showing cost-per-resolved-conversation across providers. Joins `llm_cost_daily` (costs) with `conversation_traces` (unique conversations per provider) to compute per-provider cost efficiency including avg response time.

### Files changed
- `src/assistant/data/settings.json` — Added Ollama $0 pricing entries, monthlyBudgetAlertUsd ($50), monthlyBudgetAlertEmail config
- `src/routes/admin/analytics-cost.ts` — NEW: comprehensive cost analytics endpoint (dashboard, CSV export, provider comparison, budget check)
- `src/routes/admin/index.ts` — Registered analytics-cost routes + added to semi-stable cache paths
- `shared/schema-tables.ts` — llm_usage_log table already existed from prior iteration
- `src/assistant/llm-cost-budget.ts` — checkMonthlyBudgetThreshold, insertUsageLog, queryProviderComparison already existed from prior iteration

### Patterns discovered
- Previous spiral iteration had already added most US-918 infrastructure to llm-cost-budget.ts (per-call logging, monthly budget check) and schema-tables.ts (llm_usage_log table), but the admin dashboard routes were missing
- The `analytics-cost.ts` route uses `llm_cost_daily` for aggregated cost queries (efficient) and `conversation_traces` for per-provider conversation counts (join pattern)
- CSV export uses raw SQL to avoid ORM overhead for large exports

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). Test suite: 85 passing (no regression). Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-960 Support BSUID as primary contact identifier in webhook handling
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Enhanced `extractBsuid()` in `instance.ts` to check `ExternalUserId` field (the new WhatsApp username rollout identifier from June 2026). Added `stripBsuidPrefix()` to handle `whatsapp:CC.BSUID` format from Cloud API alongside Baileys' raw `CC.BSUID` format. ExternalUserId is checked first (highest priority), followed by msg.lid, msg.key.participant, and the from JID itself.
- **AC2**: Already implemented in US-477 — `conversationKey()` in conversation-db.ts falls back gracefully from phone to BSUID-prefixed key when `from` is not a phone number (fewer than 7 digits).
- **AC3**: Added BSUID-aware outbound messaging in `WhatsAppManager.sendMessage()` and `sendInteractiveMessage()`. When a BSUID-keyed phone is detected (via `isBsuidKey()`), `resolveBsuidToJid()` looks up the phone number from the conversations table within the 30-day window, falling back to direct BSUID delivery if no phone mapping exists.
- **AC4**: Already implemented in US-477 — `mergeBsuidConversation()` in conversation-db.ts migrates messages from BSUID-only records to phone-keyed records when both identifiers are seen together.
- **AC5**: Added `bsuidLinkedAt` timestamp column to `rainbowConversations` schema. Updated `upsertConversation()` to set this timestamp when both phone and BSUID are present on the same message. Added `lookupPhoneByBsuidWithWindow()` that checks the 30-day window before returning a phone mapping. Expired mappings are logged but not deleted (for audit trail).
- **AC6**: Created `ensurePhoneForAuthTemplate()` in `bsuid-resolver.ts` — validates that a phone number (not BSUID) is available before sending authentication templates. Returns null if only BSUID is known, preventing auth template delivery to BSUID-only contacts.

### Files changed
- `src/lib/whatsapp/instance.ts` — Enhanced `extractBsuid()` with ExternalUserId support and `stripBsuidPrefix()` helper
- `shared/schema-tables.ts` — Added `bsuidLinkedAt` timestamp column to `rainbowConversations`
- `src/assistant/conversation-db.ts` — Updated `upsertConversation()` for bsuidLinkedAt tracking; added `lookupPhoneByBsuidWithWindow()` helper
- `src/lib/bsuid-resolver.ts` — NEW: BSUID resolution utilities (`isBsuidKey`, `resolveBsuidToJid`, `ensurePhoneForAuthTemplate`)
- `src/lib/whatsapp/manager.ts` — Updated `sendMessage()` and `sendInteractiveMessage()` to resolve BSUID keys before delivery

### Patterns discovered
- Existing US-477 BSUID support covers ~60% of US-960 requirements — conversation keying, merge logic, and DB schema were already in place
- The WhatsApp Manager is the single outbound gateway — all sendMessage calls route through it, making it the ideal BSUID resolution point
- Baileys uses @lid JIDs internally for LID-mapped contacts, separate from BSUIDs — the LidMapper handles @lid resolution while BSUID resolver handles the higher-level identity resolution

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). Server starts cleanly. Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-918 AI provider token consumption and cost tracking per conversation
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Every AI provider API call now logs a structured JSON record with: provider, model, input_tokens, output_tokens, estimated_cost_usd, conversation_id, tenant_id, timestamp. Per-call records are also persisted to new `llm_usage_log` DB table for historical analysis.
- **AC2**: Admin dashboard endpoints enhanced: `GET /analytics/llm-cost/monthly?month=YYYY-MM` returns monthly spend by provider, spend by tenant, average cost per conversation. Existing daily/weekly endpoints already existed from US-433.
- **AC3**: Monthly budget alert ($50 USD default) checks in the 30-minute interval alongside daily budget check. Configurable via `costBudget.monthlyBudgetAlertUsd` in settings.json. Triggers admin notification when monthly spend exceeds threshold.
- **AC4**: `GET /analytics/llm-cost/export?format=csv&month=YYYY-MM` returns CSV download of per-call usage data. Also supports `format=json`, `days=N`, and `tenant_id` filter.

## Iteration 56 - Story: US-946 - Implement OWASP LLM05 improper output handling sanitisation
### What was implemented
- Created `src/assistant/output-sanitizer.ts` — server-side output sanitization module with:
  - `escapeHtml()` — full HTML entity escaping for HTML contexts
  - `stripDangerousHtml()` — selective removal of dangerous patterns (script, iframe, event handlers, javascript: URIs, data:text/html, etc.)
  - `sanitizeWebchatOutput()` — combined strip + escape for webchat widget
  - `sanitizeToolError()` — generic error messages that don't leak internal schema/argument details
- Integrated `stripDangerousHtml()` into all webchat output paths:
  - Non-streaming JSON response in `webchat-api.ts`
  - Streaming SSE tokens in `chat-stream.ts` (Groq, Gemini, OpenAI-compatible)
  - Static SSE responses via `sendStaticSSE()`
- Updated tool validation error handling in `ai-response-generator.ts` to use `sanitizeToolError()` — detailed errors logged server-side, only generic message sent to LLM/user
- Created comprehensive test suite (`output-sanitizer.test.ts`) with 23 tests:
  - Output sanitization: escapeHtml, stripDangerousHtml, sanitizeWebchatOutput
  - 7 adversarial tool payload tests: SQL injection (DROP TABLE, UNION SELECT, DELETE), shell injection ($(), backticks, pipe to bash), path traversal
  - Tool error sanitization: verifies no internal structure leakage

### Patterns discovered
- Codebase already had strong defenses: validateToolArgs (US-928), parameterized SQL everywhere, prompt injection detection (US-422/US-928)
- Main gap was output-side: no sanitization of LLM responses before sending to webchat widget
- Streaming tokens are sanitized per-chunk; cross-chunk patterns (e.g. `<scr` + `ipt>`) are unlikely but theoretically possible — client-side defense-in-depth recommended
- Tool validation errors previously included schema details (argument names, types) — now generic

### Gotchas
- Pre-existing test failure in config-integration.integration.test.ts (service_request action) — not related to this change
- Global regex patterns need `lastIndex = 0` reset before each test to avoid stateful matching issues

### Patterns discovered
- Owner's phone number is whitelisted via flexible matching (with/without country code, spaces, dashes)

- Raw KB content is still saved to DB for admin viewing; only the LLM context cache is masked

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-977 - Implement webhook event deduplication to prevent double-processing
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: Created `src/lib/webhook-dedup.ts` — in-memory TTL cache (300s / 5 min) using a `Map<string, {expiresAt}>`. `isDuplicateMessage(wamid)` deduplicates inbound messages by wamid. First call records the entry; second call returns `true` (duplicate).
- **AC2**: Duplicates are logged at `console.debug` level in both the message-status handler and the new inbound messages handler. No error is returned to Meta — always 200.
- **AC3**: `isDuplicateStatus(wamid, status)` deduplicates status updates by `status:wamid:status` composite key. Different status values for the same wamid (e.g., `delivered` then `read`) are NOT treated as duplicates.
- **AC4**: Pure in-memory `Map` with TTL entry eviction — no NodeCache or Redis dependency needed. Periodic cleanup interval (`setInterval`) runs every 300s and is `unref`'d so it doesn't block process exit.
- **AC5**: `getDedupStats()` returns `{ dedupHits, cacheSize }` — exposed via `GET /webhooks/meta/dedup-stats` endpoint. `resetDedupHitCount()` allows period resets.

### Files changed
- `src/lib/webhook-dedup.ts` — NEW: TTL dedup cache with `isDuplicateMessage`, `isDuplicateStatus`, `getDedupStats`, `resetDedupHitCount`
- `src/routes/webhooks/index.ts` — Added `isDuplicateMessage`/`isDuplicateStatus` import; added dedup check to message-status handler; added new `POST /webhooks/meta/messages` endpoint with wamid dedup; added `GET /webhooks/meta/messages` for Meta webhook verification; added `GET /webhooks/meta/dedup-stats` metrics endpoint
- `src/routes/webhooks/__tests__/webhook-dedup.test.ts` — NEW: 14 tests covering message dedup, status dedup, namespace isolation, stats, and reset

### Patterns discovered
- NodeCache is NOT installed — simple `Map<string, {expiresAt}>` pattern is sufficient and avoids a dependency
- Cleanup interval must be `unref`'d to prevent it from blocking `timeout 10` server startup tests
- Message and status caches must use different key prefixes (`msg:` vs `status:`) to avoid cross-contamination
- Meta webhook GET verification requires `META_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN` env var; falls back to empty string (403 on mismatch)

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 14/14 dedup tests pass. Server starts. All 5 acceptance criteria verified. Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-970 - Implement AI chatbot identity disclosure and anti-impersonation guard
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: First outbound message in every new conversation includes AI disclosure. Implemented via `!convo.aiDisclosed` check in input-validator.ts after `getOrCreate`. Disclosure sent before user message is processed; `convo.aiDisclosed = true` prevents repeat disclosure in same session. Sent as a prepended message before the pipeline continues.
- **AC2**: Direct identity questions ("are you a bot?", "who am I talking to?", etc.) are intercepted in input-validator before pipeline runs. 12 regex patterns in 4 languages (EN/MS/ZH/TA). Returns truthful response and `{ continue: false }`.
- **AC3**: AI never claims to be human — default `getIdentityTruthResponse()` text explicitly states "not a human". System prompt already enforces this for LLM responses.
- **AC4**: After 3 consecutive failed intents → human escalation — already implemented by the `unknownCount` tiered escalation system (consecutive_fallback_threshold=2, escalateAt=3).
- **AC5**: Disclosure text is configurable per property via `PATCH /api/rainbow/settings` with `identity_disclosure.disclosure_text` and `identity_disclosure.identity_truth` objects per language. Defaults in `settings.json` for both Pelangi and Southern.
- **AC6**: `logDisclosureAudit()` writes to `compliance_audit_log` table with intent `ai_identity_disclosure_opening` or `ai_identity_disclosure_query`. Fire-and-forget pattern consistent with other compliance logging.

### Files changed
- `src/assistant/identity-disclosure.ts` — NEW: `isIdentityQuestion()` regex engine (12 patterns, 4 languages), `getDisclosureText()`, `getIdentityTruthResponse()`, `logDisclosureAudit()`
- `src/assistant/types.ts` — Added `aiDisclosed: boolean` to `ConversationState` interface

### Patterns discovered
- Chinese CJK regex must NOT use `\b` word boundaries — they don't match at CJK character positions; just remove `\b` around Chinese patterns
- `input-validator.ts` is the right insertion point for identity disclosure: it runs before all intent classification and before the user message is logged, allowing the disclosure to be sent as the first message
- `convo.aiDisclosed` persists for the session TTL (1 hour), preventing repeated disclosures in the same session
- `compliance_audit_log` accepts `intent` as free text — use descriptive intent strings like `ai_identity_disclosure_opening`

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-938 - LLM reliability: exponential backoff with jitter on AI provider timeouts
### What was implemented
- **AC1**: `retryProviderChat` wrapper retries transient errors (HTTP 429, 503, TimeoutError) with exponential backoff: 1s, 2s, 4s (max 3 retries per provider) before failing over
- **AC2**: Each retry delay includes ±20% random jitter via `calculateBackoffDelay()` to avoid synchronized retries from concurrent conversations
- **AC3**: After max retries exhausted on a provider, `retryProviderChat` throws and `chatWithFallback` fails over to the next provider in the chain
- **AC4**: Permanent errors (HTTP 400, 401, 422) bypass retry completely — `isPermanentError()` triggers immediate failover
- **AC5**: Retry attempt count and total latency logged per conversation via `RetryStats` object. On success after retries: logged at info level. On exhaustion: logged at warn level with retry stats JSON
- **AC6**: 24 Vitest tests covering: transient/permanent error classification, jitter bounds, retry behavior for 429/503, no-retry for 400/401/422, retry stats logging, and full chatWithFallback integration

### Files changed
- `src/assistant/ai-provider-manager.ts` — Added `isTransientError`, `isPermanentError`, `calculateBackoffDelay`, `backoffUtils.sleep`, `retryProviderChat`, `RetryStats` type. Replaced direct `providerChat` call in `_chatWithFallbackInner` with `retryProviderChat` + retry stats logging
- `src/assistant/__tests__/exponential-backoff.test.ts` — NEW: 24 tests for exponential backoff with jitter
- `src/assistant/__tests__/latency-failover.test.ts` — Updated: mock `backoffUtils.sleep` in existing tests, updated timeout test expectations to account for retries (4 calls per provider instead of 1)

### Patterns discovered
- ESM module spy limitation: `vi.spyOn(module, 'namedExport')` doesn't intercept internal calls within the module. Fix: use mutable object pattern (`backoffUtils.sleep`) so the spy replaces the method reference
- `withTimeout` race pattern conflicts with `setTimeout` mocking — must isolate backoff sleep from timeout mechanisms
- Existing latency-failover tests needed updating since TimeoutError is now transient (retried) instead of immediate failover

### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 32/32 tests pass (24 new + 8 existing updated). Server starts. All 6 acceptance criteria verified. Story marked passes=true.

## Pre-analysis for US-965 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This user story appears to describe a security pipeline for a TypeScript-based AI application, not a Frappe/Python app. The file list (`.ts` files) and concepts (RAG, embeddings) are not typical for a payroll integration.

However, transposing these requirements into a Frappe/Python context for an app like `lhdn_payroll_integration` would look like this:

**Technical Brief: US-965 Frappe Implementation**

1.  **Python Files to Modify:**
    *   `.../api/kb_ingest.py` (new): A whitelisted API endpoint to handle markdown file ingestion.
    *   `.../doctype/kb_document/kb_document.py`: Controller logic to trigger hashing and embedding on save.
    *   `.../tasks/audit.py` (new): Contains the logic for the nightly embedding anomaly detection audit.
    *   `.../hooks.py`: To register the `daily` scheduler event that runs the audit task.

2.  **Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**
    *   **DocTypes (New):**
        *   `KB Document`: Stores content, SHA-256 hash, and vector embedding.
        *   `KB File Source`: Stores original file path and hash to detect content changes.
        *   `KB Ingest Audit`: Logs all create, update, and delete events with user identity.
    *   **Hooks:**
        *   `scheduler_events`: Define a `"daily"` event to trigger the `tasks.audit.run_embedding_audit` method.

3.  **Implementation Approach:**
    *   The `kb_ingest` API will be the sole entry point for adding/updating documents. It will validate the source path against an allowlist in `site_config.json`.
    *   On document save (`on_update`), the `KB Document` controller will calculate the SHA-256 hash and compare it with the stored hash in `KB File Source`. If it mismatches, the save is rejected pending an "override" flag from an admin.
    *   Use `frappe.enqueue` to offload embedding generation (using `sentence-transformers`) to a background worker.
    *   The nightly audit task will fetch all embeddings, recalculate cluster centroids, flag documents with significant cosine similarity drift, and create a `ToDo` for the system administrator.

4.  **Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
    *   **Dependencies:** `numpy`, `scikit-learn`, and `sentence-transformers` must be added to `requirements.txt`, which can complicate bench updates.
    *   **Performance:** Embedding generation is slow. Ensure it's always a background job and consider batching.
    *   **Baseline State:** The initial "baseline" for embeddings must be established and stored securely, perhaps as a signed fixture or a private file asset.



## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-991 Inbound webhook idempotency guard using message-ID deduplication
### What was implemented
- **AC1 (Dedup within 24h)**: Added `isDuplicateMessage()` using Redis SETNX (`SET key 1 NX EX ttl`) — atomic check-and-set with configurable TTL (default 24h). Second delivery of same messageId is ACK'd but not enqueued
- **AC2 (Dedup key is messages[].id)**: Uses `msg.messageId` field from `IncomingMessage` as the dedup key, prefixed with `rainbow:dedup:` in Redis
- **AC3 (Configurable TTL)**: `dedup_ttl_seconds` field added to `message_queue` section of `settings.json` (default 86400 = 24h). Loaded in `src/assistant/index.ts` via `setDedupTtl()`
- **AC4 (Vitest test)**: 11 tests covering all 5 ACs — two identical payloads assert only one passes through, second triggers dedup log
- **AC5 (Dedup logged)**: `console.log('[MessageQueue] Dedup: skipping duplicate messageId=...')` emitted for every skipped duplicate
### Files changed
- `src/lib/message-queue.ts` — Added ioredis import, `isDuplicateMessage()` with Redis SETNX + in-memory fallback, `setDedupTtl()`, `_testExports`, in-memory dedup Map with TTL expiry and size limit (2000 entries). Updated `enqueueMessage()` with dedup guard at top, `closeQueue()` to clean up Redis client and memory dedup
- `src/assistant/index.ts` — Import `setDedupTtl`, wire up `dedup_ttl_seconds` from settings before `initMessageQueue`
- `src/assistant/data/settings.json` — Added `dedup_ttl_seconds: 86400` to `message_queue` section with updated description
- `src/assistant/__tests__/message-dedup.test.ts` — NEW: 11 tests covering all 5 acceptance criteria
### Patterns discovered
- BullMQ already uses ioredis under the hood but doesn't expose its Redis client for SETNX — a separate ioredis client is needed for the dedup guard
- In-memory fallback is essential since Redis may be unavailable (existing pattern from US-405)
- The existing Baileys-level dedup in instance.ts covers 60s double-fires; this new guard covers 24h webhook retries across restarts

## Pre-analysis for US-965 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
There is a discrepancy in your request. The user story and project context you've provided relate to a **TypeScript/Node.js** application named `rainbow-ai`, focused on a RAG pipeline for an AI assistant. The request, however, asks for a brief for a **Frappe/Python** app.

The following technical brief is based on the provided JSON and file structure, which belong to the TypeScript project, as a Frappe/Python analysis would not be relevant to the source material.

***

### **Technical Brief: US-965 - RAG Knowledge Base Integrity Pipeline**

This brief outlines the implementation strategy for securing the RAG knowledge base ingestion pipeline in the `rainbow-ai` TypeScript project.

1.  **Files to Modify/Create:**
    *   `shared/schema-tables.ts`: Define new Drizzle ORM schemas for `rainbow_kb_files` (storing file hashes) and `rainbow_config_audit` (logging ingest events).
    *   `src/assistant/services/kb-ingestion.service.ts` (Likely location): This service, responsible for reading markdown files from `.rainbow-kb/`, will be heavily modified to incorporate hashing, source validation, and audit logging.
    *   `src/lib/crypto.utils.ts` (New file): Create helpers for SHA-256 file hashing.
    *   `scripts/audit-kb-embeddings.ts` (New file): A standalone script for the nightly embedding anomaly detection audit.
    *   `ecosystem.config.cjs`: To schedule the new nightly audit script using PM2's cron feature.

2.  **Key Architectural Components Involved:**
    *   **Database Schema (Drizzle):** Two new tables are required:
        *   `rainbow_kb_files`: `(id, file_path, sha256_hash, source, last_ingested_at)`
        *   `rainbow_config_audit`: `(id, event_type, file_path, file_hash, operator_id, timestamp)`
    *   **Scheduled Job (PM2):** A cron job will be configured to execute the `audit-kb-embeddings.ts` script nightly.

3.  **Recommended Implementation Approach:**
    1.  **Schema:** Implement the new tables in `shared/schema-tables.ts` and generate the migration.
    2.  **Ingestion Pipeline:**
        *   Modify the KB ingestion service to first calculate the SHA-256 hash of a markdown file.
        *   Before embedding, query `rainbow_kb_files`. If the file path exists, compare hashes. Abort if the hash has changed, logging an error.
        *   Implement source validation by checking if the file's path matches an allowlisted prefix (e.g., local path, S3 bucket).
        *   Upon successful ingestion or a validated update, write the file path and hash to `rainbow_kb_files` and log the event to `rainbow_config_audit`.
    3.  **Nightly Audit:**
        *   The `audit-kb-embeddings.ts` script will fetch document embeddings from the vector DB.
        *   It will calculate the centroid for topic clusters and flag documents whose embedding's cosine similarity falls below a configurable threshold (e.g., < 2 std deviations from the mean).
        *   Alerts should be logged or sent to an operator.

4.  **Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
    *   **Operator Override:** A mechanism (e.g., a CLI flag or temporary environment variable) is needed for an operator to approve a hash change and force re-ingestion.
    *   **Baseline Snapshot:** The initial run of the nightly audit needs a process to create and sign a trusted baseline of the current embeddings.
    *   **Performance:** The audit script may be resource-intensive. Process embeddings in batches to manage memory and CPU load on the database and vector store.
    *   **Race Conditions:** Use database transactions when checking for and inserting file hashes to prevent issues if multiple ingestion processes run concurrently.


## Iteration 5 - Mon, Mar 16, 2026  2:30 AM
Story: US-1015 — Structured LLM output schema enforcement with Zod validation and retry
### What was implemented
- **AC1 (JSON schema to provider)**: Added `zod-to-json-schema` package. `providerChat()` and `chatWithFallback()` accept optional `jsonSchema` param, passed as `response_format: { type: 'json_schema', json_schema: {...} }` to OpenAI-compatible and Groq providers. `zodSchemaToJsonSchema()` helper converts Zod schemas to JSON Schema format. Falls back to `json_object` mode if schema conversion fails.
- **AC2 (Max 2 retries with corrective prompt)**: `generateWithValidation()` upgraded from 1 retry to 2 retries (default). Each retry appends a corrective user message containing the JSON schema description and specific validation errors, enabling the LLM to self-correct.
- **AC3 (Safe fallback)**: All call sites (`classifyAndRespond`, `classifyIntent`, `classifyOnly`, `generateReplyOnly`) now route through `generateWithValidation()`. When all retries exhaust, each caller applies its own safe fallback: unknown intent with 0 confidence for classifiers, static fallback messages for reply generators. Raw JSON is never propagated to the guest.
- **AC4 (Failure logging)**: `logStructuredOutputFailure()` writes `structured_output_failure` events to `intent_predictions` table via `trackIntentPrediction()` with schema name, attempt number, and error details. Logged asynchronously (non-blocking).
- **AC5 (Vitest tests)**: 13 tests covering: valid schema pass on first attempt, single failure with retry success, all retries exhausted (null data), failure event logging, JSON schema passthrough to provider, no-content handling, corrective prompt includes schema, maxRetries=0 skips retries, providerIds passthrough.
### Files changed
- `package.json` — Added `zod-to-json-schema` dependency
- `src/assistant/ai-provider-manager.ts` — `providerChat()` and `chatWithFallback()` accept optional `jsonSchema` param; Groq and OpenAI-compatible branches pass `json_schema` response_format when provided
- `src/assistant/ai-response-generator.ts` — Import `zodToJsonSchema`; new `zodSchemaToJsonSchema()` and `logStructuredOutputFailure()` helpers; `generateWithValidation()` rewritten with 2-retry loop, JSON schema enforcement, corrective prompting, and failure logging; `classifyAndRespond()` uses 2 retries + schema name; `generateReplyOnly()` wired through `generateWithValidation()`
- `src/assistant/ai-classification.ts` — `classifyIntent()` and `classifyOnly()` wired through `generateWithValidation()` with provider IDs passthrough
- `src/assistant/__tests__/structured-output-enforcement.test.ts` — NEW: 13 tests covering all 5 ACs
### Patterns discovered
- `zod-to-json-schema` with `target: 'openApi3'` produces clean JSON Schema from Zod that OpenAI-compatible providers accept
- Not all providers support `json_schema` response_format (some only support `json_object`), so the schema is best-effort enhancement over the existing json_object mode
- `generateWithValidation` now serves as the single enforcement point for all structured LLM outputs — future structured calls should use it
- Provider IDs need to be passed through to allow T4 provider selection in classification
### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 13/13 new tests pass + 14/14 existing LLM validation tests pass. All 5 acceptance criteria verified. Story marked passes=true.

## Iteration 6 - Sun, Mar 16, 2026  2:51 AM
Story: US-965 — Implement OWASP LLM04 RAG knowledge base integrity validation pipeline
### What was implemented
- **AC1 (SHA-256 hashing)**: `computeContentHash()` in `kb-integrity.ts` computes SHA-256 hex digests. `saveKBFileWithHash()` in `config-db.ts` stores the hash in `rainbow_kb_files.sha256_hash`. Re-ingestion is rejected (file marked `approved=FALSE`) when content hash changes without explicit operator approval via `approveKBFile()`.
- **AC2 (Source validation)**: `validateSource()` checks file paths against allowlisted local directories and S3 prefixes. `initAllowedSources()` configures the allowlist at startup. Rejects documents from unverified sources.
- **AC3 (Nightly embedding audit)**: `kb-embedding-audit.ts` implements `createBaseline()` to snapshot per-file embedding centroids with SHA-256 signature, and `runNightlyAudit()` to compare current embeddings against baseline and alert operator on anomalies.
- **AC4 (Embedding anomaly detection)**: `detectAnomalies()` computes cosine distance of each file's centroid from the global centroid and flags files exceeding configurable threshold (default: 2 standard deviations from mean).
- **AC5 (Audit logging)**: `auditKBEvent()` in `config-db.ts` writes all KB ingest events (add, update, delete, hash_changed, source_rejected, approved, embedding_anomaly) to `rainbow_config_audit` with operator identity and file hash.
### Root cause of previous failures
Previous 2 attempts created `kb-integrity.ts` and `kb-embedding-audit.ts` that imported functions (`saveKBFileWithHash`, `approveKBFile`, `auditKBEvent`, `getUnapprovedKBFiles`) from `config-db.ts` — but those functions were never added to `config-db.ts`. The schema columns (`sha256_hash`, `source`, `approved`, `last_ingested_at`, `ingested_by`) were also missing from the `ensureConfigTables()` DDL.
### Files changed
- `src/lib/config-db.ts` — Added US-965 schema columns to `rainbow_kb_files` table DDL; added `saveKBFileWithHash()`, `approveKBFile()`, `auditKBEvent()`, `getUnapprovedKBFiles()` functions
### Patterns discovered
- When previous iterations create files that import non-existent functions, the fix is to add the missing functions rather than rewrite everything
- The `config-db.ts` pattern uses `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` for idempotent schema evolution
- All DB functions follow fail-open pattern (return safe defaults when DATABASE_URL not set)
### Status
✅ Build passes (3 pre-existing warnings). 15/15 tests pass. Server starts. All 5 acceptance criteria verified.

## Pre-analysis for US-966 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
**Technical Brief: US-966 Vector Store Access Control**

This brief outlines the implementation of OWASP LLM06 principles within the Frappe/Python environment, translating the user story's requirements for multi-tenancy and security.

**1. Python Files to Modify:**
*   `[app_name]/api.py`: The primary file for exposing the vector search endpoint. All search logic should be gated here.
*   `[app_name]/vector_store/client.py` (or similar): A new or existing module to abstract the vector database interactions (e.g., pgvector, Pinecone, Chroma). This module will handle adding the mandatory tenant filter to all queries.
*   `[app_name]/hooks.py`: To define a daily or weekly scheduled job for the audit script and potentially `on_save` hooks for DocTypes that are indexed.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**
*   **DocTypes:**
    *   A new DocType `Vector Query Log` should be created to store audit trail information (user, query, tenant ID, retrieved doc IDs).
    *   The `Company` DocType will likely serve as the logical partition key (`property_id` in the story).
*   **Hooks:**
    *   `doc_events`: An `on_save` hook for any knowledge base DocType to trigger re-embedding and indexing, ensuring the `company` field is passed as metadata.
    *   `scheduler_events`: A `daily` or `weekly` event to trigger the `run_partition_audit` script.

**3. Implementation Approach:**
1.  All vector search functions in `client.py` must be modified to require a `company` argument.
2.  This `company` argument will be used to apply a mandatory metadata filter on every vector database query.
3.  The main `@frappe.whitelist()` search endpoint in `api.py` will retrieve the user's session company and pass it to the search function. It must reject requests where the user's company is not set.
4.  Upon every query, create a new `Vector Query Log` document to satisfy the audit requirement.
5.  Implement the scheduled audit script to fetch sample vectors from different companies and verify no cross-tenant results are returned.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Data Backfill:** A migration script is required to update all existing embeddings in the vector store with the correct `company` metadata field.
*   **Permissions:** Ensure API endpoints strictly enforce permissions, as users may have access to multiple companies. The query must be scoped to only one company at a time.
*   **Raw Embeddings:** Do not expose the raw embedding vectors in any API response to mitigate embedding inversion attacks.

*   **Data Backfilling:** A migration script will be required to tag all existing embeddings in the database with the correct `property_id`.

*   `src/lib/vectorDbClient.ts` or `src/assistant/retrieval.ts`: Any file responsible for querying the vector database (e.g., pgvector). Logic here must be updated to use the `property_id`.
*   `shared/schema-tables.ts`: The Drizzle/Prisma schema file defining the embeddings table. This is the equivalent of a Frappe DocType schema.
*   `src/routes/assistant/query.ts`: The API endpoint handler that receives user queries. This is where authentication and `property_id` context should originate.
*   `scripts/audit-vector-store.ts`: A new script for the quarterly audit requirement.

**2. DocTypes / Hooks Involved:**
*   **DocType (Schema):** The database table schema for embeddings (in `shared/schema-tables.ts`) must be modified to include a `property_id` or `namespace` text column to partition data.
*   **Hooks (Middleware):** An authentication middleware needs to verify the service identity and attach the corresponding `property_id` to the request object for downstream use.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  **Schema:** Add a `property_id` column to the embeddings table. Create a migration script to backfill this value for existing embeddings.
2.  **Filtering:** Modify all vector search functions to require a `property_id` and add a `WHERE property_id = ?` clause to the SQL query. Ensure the column is indexed.
3.  **Authentication:** Update the query API endpoint to enforce authentication. Extract `property_id` from the authenticated principal (e.g., JWT payload) and pass it to the search function.
4.  **Logging:** In the search function, add structured logging to record the principal, query, `property_id`, and retrieved document IDs for the audit trail.
5.  **Auditing:** Develop a script that runs test queries for each property and asserts that no results from other properties are returned.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Data Migration:** Accurately mapping existing embeddings to the correct `property_id` during the backfill is critical to prevent initial data contamination.
*   **API Security:** Ensure raw embedding vectors are never exposed in any API response to mitigate embedding inversion attacks.
*   **Performance:** The new `property_id` column in the vector table must be indexed to prevent slow queries.

## Pre-analysis for US-968 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This is a TypeScript/Node.js project, not Frappe/Python. Based on the user story and file structure, here is the technical brief:

### Technical Brief: US-968 PDPA Compliance

**1. Python Files to Modify (Node.js/TypeScript Equivalents):**
*   `shared/schema-tables.ts`: To add an `encrypted` flag or type for PII fields (guest names, phone, booking data).
*   `src/lib/db.ts` (or equivalent): To wrap database query functions, transparently handling encryption/decryption of PII fields using a new crypto utility module.
*   `src/routes/middleware/rbac.ts` (new file): To create a Role-Based Access Control middleware for gating access to PII.
*   `src/routes/*.ts`: Apply the new RBAC middleware to all routes that handle guest or booking data.
*   `src/jobs/retention.ts` (new file): To house the logic for the automated data retention/anonymization cron job.
*   `scripts/migrations/encrypt-existing-pii.ts` (new file): A one-time script to migrate and encrypt existing PII in the database.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks (Equivalent Concepts):**
*   **DocTypes:** The schemas defined in `shared/schema-tables.ts` for `guests`, `bookings`, and `users`/`roles` are the primary "DocTypes" involved.
*   **Hooks:** A new RBAC middleware will function as a "hook" on the Express/Fastify routes to check permissions before the controller logic is executed. A scheduled job (e.g., using `node-cron`) will serve as a "cron hook" for data retention.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
*   **Encryption:** Use Node.js's built-in `crypto` library to implement AES-256-GCM encryption. Store the encryption key securely as an environment variable (`ENCRYPTION_KEY`). The database utility will check the schema for encrypted fields and automatically encrypt/decrypt them on write/read.
*   **RBAC:** The middleware will check a user's role against a predefined list of roles authorized for PII access. If unauthorized, it will either return a `403 Forbidden` error or strip PII fields from the response.
*   **Data Retention:** A nightly cron job will query for guest records where `checkout_date` is older than 2 years and either delete them or update PII fields to an anonymized value (e.g., 'DELETED').
*   **Documentation:** Create `docs/DPA.md` and `docs/Data_Processing_Inventory.md`.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Key Management:** The `ENCRYPTION_KEY` is critical. If lost, all encrypted data is lost. It must not be committed to git.
*   **Data Migration:** The migration script for existing data must be robust, idempotent, and tested on a staging environment first to prevent data loss.
*   **Performance:** On-the-fly encryption/decryption adds overhead. This may impact performance on routes with large data sets.
*   **Search/Indexing:** Encrypted fields cannot be directly searched or indexed by the database. Any search functionality on PII fields will require a different approach (e.g., searching on a separate, hashed, or partially-unencrypted field if absolutely necessary and compliant).

### Technical Implementation Brief

This brief outlines the required changes in a Frappe/Python environment to implement a WhatsApp double opt-in workflow. The story context implies a marketing/CRM function, not payroll, so the file paths should be adapted to the relevant custom app.

**1. Python Files to Modify:**

*   `[app_name]/hooks.py`: To define document events, the incoming webhook endpoint, and the scheduled task.
*   `[app_name]/api.py`: Create this to house the whitelisted `handle_whatsapp_reply` function for processing incoming messages.
*   `[app_name]/tasks.py`: Create this for the background job logic that handles consent expiration.
*   `[app_name]/doctype/contact/contact.py`: If extending the standard `Contact` controller.
*   `[app_name]/utils/whatsapp_messaging.py`: A new utility module to encapsulate logic for sending WhatsApp messages.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**

*   **DocTypes:**
    *   `Contact`: Add a field `marketing_consent` (Select: Confirmed, Revoked, Pending Initial, Pending Reply).
    *   `WhatsApp Opt-in Log` (New DocType): Create this to store an immutable audit trail (Contact, Status, Timestamp, Source/IP).
*   **Hooks:**
    *   `doc_events`: Use `on_update` for the `Contact` DocType to trigger the initial opt-in message.
    *   `whitelisted_guest_methods`: To expose `handle_whatsapp_reply` as a public webhook endpoint for Meta.
    *   `scheduler_events`: A `daily` or `hourly` job to transition `Pending Reply` contacts to `Pending Initial` after 48 hours.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**

1.  **Schema:** Add the `marketing_consent` field to `Contact`. Create the `WhatsApp Opt-in Log` DocType for auditing.
2.  **Trigger Flow:** On `Contact` update, if a user is added to the marketing list, trigger a function that sends the pre-approved WhatsApp template via `whatsapp_messaging.py` and creates a `Pending Reply` log entry.
3.  **Webhook Handler:** The `handle_whatsapp_reply` function will parse incoming messages, find the `Contact` by phone number, and update `marketing_consent` based on keyword matching ("YES", "STOP"). It must log every event to the audit DocType.
4.  **Scheduled Job:** Implement the scheduled function to query for `Pending Reply` logs older than 48 hours and update the `Contact`'s status accordingly.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**

*   **Keyword Matching:** Handle case-insensitivity and variations (e.g., "stop.", "YES!").
*   **Idempotency:** The webhook should not create duplicate audit logs or state changes if it receives the same message multiple times.
*   **Configuration:** Store WhatsApp credentials and template IDs in a custom Settings DocType or `site_config.json`, not hardcoded.
*   **Error Handling:** Gracefully manage failures in the WhatsApp API call and unsolicited "STOP" messages from numbers not in an active flow.

## Pre-analysis for US-992 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This user story describes a TypeScript/Node.js project, not a Frappe/Python one. The file paths (`.ts`), dependencies (`@opentelemetry/sdk-node`), and technical notes confirm this.

Here is the technical brief for implementing the story in its actual **TypeScript** environment:

**1. Files to Modify:**
*   `src/assistant/ai-provider-manager.ts`: Core logic for AI provider calls; will contain the primary `CLIENT` span instrumentation.
*   `src/assistant/ai-response-generator.ts`: Orchestrates calls; will initiate the parent trace context.
*   `src/instrumentation.ts` (New File): Centralize OpenTelemetry SDK initialization and exporter configuration.
*   `src/lib/config.ts` (or similar): To read and expose the `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` environment variable.
*   `package.json`: To add `@opentelemetry/sdk-node` and `@opentelemetry/exporter-trace-otlp-http` dependencies.

**2. Key Modules / Classes Involved:**
*   `AIProviderManager`: The class where API calls are made. Spans will be created here to wrap provider requests.
*   `AIResponseGenerator`: The entry point for the LLM pipeline, where the top-level trace should begin.
*   A new `TelemetryService` or `TracerProvider` module in `instrumentation.ts` to manage the OTEL SDK lifecycle.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  In `instrumentation.ts`, initialize the NodeSDK. Use an `OtlpHttpTraceExporter` if `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is present; otherwise, use a `NoopSpanProcessor` to disable tracing.
2.  In `ai-provider-manager.ts`, get the global tracer. Before each external AI provider call, start a span (e.g., `'chat llm/openai/gpt-4'`).
3.  Use the provider response to populate span attributes like `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` and `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons`.
4.  Wrap the provider call in a `try/catch/finally` to ensure `span.end()` is always called.
5.  The OTEL SDK's context manager should automatically link spans in the provider fallover chain under a single trace.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Async Context:** Verify that OTEL's async context propagates correctly across the promise-based retry/failover logic to ensure a unified trace.
*   **Graceful Failure:** Telemetry code must not crash the application. Wrap instrumentation in defensive `try/catch` blocks.
*   **Configuration:** The system must remain fully functional when the OTEL endpoint is not configured. The `NoopSpanProcessor` handles this.
*   **Semantic Conventions:** Adhere strictly to the GenAI Semantic Convention names (e.g., `gen_ai.system`) for backend compatibility.

## Pre-analysis for US-993 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
### Technical Brief for Claude: US-993 Groundedness Score Gate

**Project:** `rainbow-ai` (TypeScript/Node.js assistant)
**Objective:** Implement a groundedness score to prevent delivering AI responses that are not supported by the retrieved knowledge base (KB) context.

**1. Python Files to Modify:**
*This project is TypeScript, not Python.* Based on the RAG architecture described, the most likely files for modification are:
- `src/assistant/pipelines/rag-pipeline.ts` or similar: Core logic for orchestrating the Retrieve-Augment-Generate flow. The groundedness check should be inserted here, after the LLM call and before the response is finalized.
- `src/lib/llm-client.ts` or `src/assistant/llm.ts`: Wrappers for the LLM call. The response and its source context are likely available here.
- `src/lib/vector-store.ts` or similar: Logic for embedding and similarity search. A new function to compute sentence-level similarity against context chunks will be needed here.
- `src/lib/config.ts` or `shared/schema.ts`: To add the new `groundedness_threshold` configuration parameter.

**2. Involved Frappe DocTypes/Hooks:**
The equivalent in this TS project are configuration objects and data schemas:
- **Configuration:** The new `groundedness_threshold` should be added to the existing `hallucination_detection` configuration object.
- **Data Schema:** The groundedness score (a float between 0.0-1.0) should be added to the analytics/tracing schema, likely defined in `shared/schema-tables.ts`, to be stored alongside other span attributes.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  After generating the AI response, but before sending it, intercept the response text and the list of retrieved KB context chunks.
2.  Implement a function that splits the response into individual sentences/claims.
3.  For each sentence, calculate its embedding using a fast, local model.
4.  For each retrieved KB chunk, calculate its embedding (these may already be available).
5.  Calculate the cosine similarity between each sentence embedding and all KB chunk embeddings. The highest similarity score for a sentence is its "support score".
6.  The final `groundedness_score` is the fraction of sentences whose support score exceeds a similarity floor (e.g., > 0.7).
7.  If `groundedness_score < config.groundedness_threshold` (default 0.6), discard the response and trigger the existing fallback mechanism (`hallucination_action`).
8.  Log the `groundedness_score` to the trace span for analytics.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
- **Performance:** The entire check must be < 200ms. Use a lightweight, local embedding model (e.g., from `sentence-transformers` via an ONNX runtime) to avoid network latency. Do not make an external API call for the check itself.
- **Sentence Tokenization:** Simple string splitting on periods can be unreliable. Use a proper NLP sentence tokenizer to handle abbreviations and complex sentences correctly.
- **Threshold Tuning:** The `0.6` threshold is a starting point. The new dashboard histogram will be critical for observing the distribution of scores and tuning this value to balance safety and helpfulness.
- **Empty/Non-Factual Responses:** The logic must gracefully handle responses without factual claims (e.g., "I'm sorry, I don't know"). Such responses should likely bypass the check or be assigned a neutral score.


## Iteration 15 - Story: US-993 Groundedness Score Gate
### What was implemented
- `src/assistant/groundedness-checker.ts`: New module — `computeGroundednessScore()` uses sentence-level Jaccard word-overlap (deterministic, no API call, < 50ms)
- Sentences split from response, each scored against KB sentences; grounded if max overlap >= 0.15 (factual) or 0.05 (non-factual)
- `logGroundednessEvent()` async DB logger to new `groundedness_events` table (created via pool.query IF NOT EXISTS)
- `getGroundednessHistogram()` for admin analytics with histogram buckets, P50/P10 stats, daily breakdown
- `src/assistant/hallucination-detector.ts`: Added `groundedness_threshold?: number` (default 0.6) to `HallucinationConfig`
- `src/assistant/pipeline/response-processor.ts`: Groundedness gate runs after hallucination check, only if response wasn't already blocked. Reads threshold from `halluSettings.groundedness_threshold`. Blocks + escalates when score < threshold.
- `src/routes/admin/groundedness-report.ts`: New admin routes — GET histogram/stats + GET paginated events
- `src/routes/admin/index.ts`: Registered groundedness report routes
### Patterns discovered
- Jaccard word-overlap is fast and sufficient for topic-grounding (not fact-checking). For <0.15 overlap, sentence is clearly off-topic vs KB.
- Non-factual sentences (greetings, transitions) use a lower bar (0.05) to avoid false positives
- Groundedness is about topic coverage; hallucination detector covers value contradictions
### Gotchas
- Completely unrelated hallucinations (luxury spa, rooftop pool not in KB) produce Jaccard ~0.05-0.1 → correctly blocked
- Sentences that happen to share words with KB (check-in/check-out) score ~0.5+ even with wrong values → groundedness passes, hallucination detector catches the specific wrong value
- Threshold=0 disables the groundedness gate (escape hatch in config)

This brief outlines the implementation plan for the PDPA biometric consent feature in the `lhdn_payroll_integration` app.
