*   **Create New DocType:** `Biometric Consent Log`.
    *   Fields: `employee` (Link: Employee), `consent_granted` (Checkbox), `consent_timestamp` (Datetime). This DocType will be the auditable record of consent.
*   **Modify DocType `Employee`:** The OCR logic is likely triggered here.
*   **Modify Python Controller `employee.py`:** This is the most probable location for the core OCR processing logic that needs to be updated.
*   **Modify `hooks.py`:** Potentially needed to add a `doc_events` hook for the `Employee` doctype (e.g., `before_save`) to trigger the consent check if not already in `employee.py`.

**2. Recommended Implementation Approach:**

1.  In the Python function that handles the ID image upload/processing for an employee, insert a consent check *before* any API calls.
2.  The check should query the `Biometric Consent Log` for a valid entry corresponding to the `employee`.
3.  If consent is missing, halt the OCR process and use `frappe.throw()` or `frappe.msgprint()` to notify the user that explicit consent is required. Do not proceed.
4.  If consent is present, proceed with the API call. The image must be handled in-memory and passed directly to the Gemini API. **Do not** save the raw image file to the database or server filesystem.
5.  Only the structured text returned from the OCR process should be saved to the relevant fields in the `Employee` document.

**3. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**

*   **File Handling:** Frappe's default behavior is to save uploaded files. The logic must intercept the file stream *before* it's written to disk. This may require a custom endpoint or careful handling of the `before_save` event.
*   **User Experience:** The process of *obtaining* consent is not fully defined. A simple approach is a custom button on the `Employee` form ("Record Manual Consent") that a privileged user can click to create the log entry.
*   **Consent Revocation:** The story doesn't cover revoking consent. While not required now, be aware that this is a future consideration.
*   **Error States:** If the OCR API call fails *after* consent is confirmed, ensure the user is clearly notified of the processing failure.


## Iteration 16 - Story: US-1012 Multi-turn LLM Context Loss Detection and Recovery
### What was implemented
- `src/assistant/context-loss-detector.ts`: New module with 3 heuristic signals:
  1. **Re-asking**: regex patterns detect if bot asks for info user already gave (name, date, count, room type, contact)
  2. **Contradiction**: compares RM prices in response vs assistant history (>20% diff = contradiction)
  3. **Entity omission**: detects if user-mentioned booking refs (regex `[A-Z]{2,4}[0-9]{4,8}`) are absent from response
- Confidence: each signal = 0.75; min 1.0. Threshold = 0.7 so any single signal triggers detection.
- `buildRegroundingMessage()`: extracts name/date/count/room-type/booking-refs from history → compact summary string
- `logContextRecoveryEvent()`: logs `context_recovery` intent to `intent_predictions` table (fire-and-forget)
- `src/assistant/pipeline/stages/summarization.ts`: Phase 3 added after token pruning:
  - Checks last assistant message for context loss (requires >= 3 messages in context)
  - Injects re-grounding message before the last user message if detected
  - `contextRecoveryInjected` flag added to `SummarizationResult`
- `src/assistant/__tests__/context-loss-detector.test.ts`: 15 tests covering all signals + edge cases
### Patterns discovered
- Regex-based patterns catch re-asking reliably; `REASKING_QUESTION_PATTERNS` array is easy to extend
- Price contradiction is sensitive to false positives (rooms at different tiers) — kept tight bounds (>20% & <1000%)
- Entity omission is most valuable for booking refs (specific format); generalising to all entities causes noise
- The re-grounding message is injected BEFORE the last user message so it acts as context preamble
### Gotchas
- Entity omission confidence needed 0.75 (not 0.35) to cross the detection threshold alone
- "No context loss" test needed clean data without prices — RM comparisons trigger false positives for legitimate multi-tier pricing scenarios
- summarization test avoided full integration (configStore/pruneContext mocking complexity); tested through detectContextLoss directly

- The guard is a contract: callers must not persist raw images — the guard documents this but cannot enforce it at runtime

## Pre-analysis for US-995 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This user story describes a feature for a Node.js/TypeScript application, not a Frappe/Python one. The analysis is based on the provided file structure for the "Rainbow" project.

**Project:** Rainbow (Node.js/TypeScript)
**User Story:** US-995: WhatsApp portfolio pacing monitor

**Technical Brief for Implementation:**

1.  **Files to Modify:**
    *   `src/lib/metaPacingService.ts` (New): To encapsulate the logic for polling Meta's Graph API endpoint (`/message_qrdls` or analytics).
    *   `src/workers/pacingMonitor.ts` (New): A background worker using `setInterval` to call `metaPacingService` every 5 minutes.
    *   `shared/schema.ts`: To add a new `pacing_log` table schema for Drizzle ORM to log pacing events.
    *   `src/routes/pacing.ts` (New): To create a backend API endpoint (e.g., `/api/pacing/status`) that the frontend can poll.
    *   `[Frontend-Path]/PacingStatusPanel.tsx` (New): A new React/frontend component for the admin dashboard.
    *   `[Frontend-Path]/BroadcastComposer.tsx` (Existing): The existing UI component for sending broadcasts needs modification to display a warning.

2.  **Frappe DocTypes/Hooks (Conceptual Equivalents):**
    *   **"DocType"**: The `pacing_log` schema definition in `shared/schema.ts` will serve as the data model, equivalent to a DocType.
    *   **"Hook"**: The scheduled task functionality will be implemented as a long-running process in the `pacingMonitor.ts` worker, not a Frappe hook.

3.  **Implementation Approach:**
    *   Create a service (`metaPacingService`) to handle Graph API requests and authentication.
    *   Implement a worker (`pacingMonitor`) to call this service on a 5-minute interval, storing the latest pacing status in a shared cache (e.g., Redis) or the database.
    *   When pacing is detected, the worker should use the Drizzle ORM to write an entry to the new `pacing_log` table.
    *   Expose the cached status via a new REST endpoint in `src/routes/pacing.ts`.
    *   Build a frontend panel that polls this endpoint to display queue depth and pacing status.
    *   Conditionally render a warning in the broadcast UI based on the same endpoint's data.

4.  **Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
    *   **Graceful Degradation:** The API polling service must handle errors gracefully (e.g., missing API scopes, network issues) and return a "status unavailable" state to the frontend.
    *   **Stale Data:** The API endpoint should include a `lastPolled` timestamp. The frontend should indicate if the data is stale (e.g., older than 10 minutes) in case the background worker fails.
    *   **Authentication:** Ensure the Graph API token is securely retrieved and has the required `whatsapp_business_management` permissions.
    *   **UI State:** Use a centralized state management solution (like React Context or Zustand) to share the pacing status between the dashboard panel and the broadcast warning to avoid redundant API calls.

## Iteration 5 - Story: US-937 AI waiter: multi-language menu item name lookup for Chinese and Malay terms
### What was implemented
- Extended `DisambiguationCandidate` interface with optional `translations?: Record<string, string>` field
- Extended `MenuItem` interface in `menu-items-store.ts` with `translations` field (persisted as JSON TEXT in Postgres)
- Added `translations` column to `menu_items` table schema + migration for existing tables (`ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`)
- Updated `createMenuItem()` and `updateMenuItem()` CRUD to read/write translations (DB + in-memory)
- Extracted `scoreName()` helper from `findMenuItemMatches()` in `menu-matcher.ts`
- Updated `findMenuItemMatches()` to search across all translation variants — best (lowest) score wins
- Updated `normalizeItems()` in `fnb-menu.ts` to pass translations through from MCP responses
- Updated admin API (`menu-items.ts`) POST to accept translations in request body, PATCH to validate translations object
- Created 15 Vitest tests in `multilingual-menu.test.ts` covering all acceptance criteria:
  - Malay term resolution (nasi goreng, nasi ayam, kopi ais, mi goreng)
  - Chinese term resolution (炒饭, 鸡饭, 冰咖啡, 炒面, 椰浆饭)
  - English still works, items without translations still work
  - Unrecognised term returns empty results
  - translations field is optional on DisambiguationCandidate
### Patterns discovered
- `scoreName()` refactor keeps single-responsibility: one function scores a name, the outer function iterates variants
- Chinese characters work naturally with Levenshtein (each char is one unit) — exact match wins with score 0
- `parseJsonObject()` helper mirrors existing `parseJsonArray()` pattern for JSON column parsing
- DB migration uses `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` to be idempotent on existing installations
### Gotchas
- The `translations` column defaults to `'{}'` (empty JSON object string) not `NULL` to avoid null-check complexity
- `MenuItemUpdate` type (Partial<...>) automatically includes translations since MenuItem has it
- Existing test suite (20 tests in menu-disambig.test.ts) passes unchanged — no regressions


## Iteration N - Story: US-995 WhatsApp portfolio pacing monitor with queue-depth visibility
### What was implemented
- Extended `src/lib/pacing-monitor.ts` (US-891) with US-995 additions:
  - `QueueDepthMetrics` interface: messagesSent24h, messagesPending, messagesDelivered, messagesFailed, volumeAvailable
  - `PacingStatusPanel` interface: combined panel data for admin dashboard
  - `fetchMessagingVolume()`: Fetches 24h messaging analytics from Meta Graph API (sent/delivered/failed/pending estimate)
  - `getPacingStatusPanel()`: Returns structured panel data with status (normal/pacing_active/degraded/unavailable) and warnings
  - `logPacingEventToAudit()`: Logs pacing state transitions (activated/resolved) to `rainbow_config_audit` via `auditConfigChange()`
  - `checkPacingStatus()` now fetches quality + volume in parallel, logs state transitions to audit
- Extended `src/routes/admin/campaign-pacing.ts`:
  - New endpoint: `GET /analytics/pacing/status` — combined pacing status panel for dashboard
  - Returns: pacingActive, status, messagesSent, messagesQueued, warning, etc.
  - Degrades gracefully (status: 'unavailable') when Meta credentials not configured
- Extended `src/routes/admin/messaging-limits.ts`:
  - `GET /analytics/messaging-limits` now includes `queueDepth` (sent/pending/delivered/failed) and `pacingWarning` for broadcast UI
### Patterns discovered
- `auditConfigChange()` from config-db.ts uses raw SQL pool — not Drizzle — for the audit log
- Pacing monitor runs independently as a setInterval loop; state is in-memory + persisted to app_settings
- The `fetchMessagingVolume` uses Meta's analytics endpoint with start/end timestamps
- Graceful degradation: 403/400 from analytics endpoint = token lacks scope → return volumeAvailable: false
### Gotchas
- Previous attempts (2 retries) were blocked — this time focused on extending existing infrastructure rather than creating new files
- Meta analytics endpoint returns data_points array per granularity period; must sum across all points
- Pending messages = sent - delivered - failed (estimated, not exact)
- Import of `auditConfigChange` from config-db.ts must use `.js` extension (NodeNext resolution)

## Iteration N - Story: US-1007 - Implement WhatsApp MM Lite API with TTL for time-sensitive promotions

### What was implemented
- `shared/schema-tables.ts`: Added `mm_lite_sends` table tracking send API path, TTL hours, expiry timestamp, delivery status, and Meta message ID for A/B benchmarking
- `src/lib/mm-lite.ts`: Core MM Lite service with:
  - `getMmLiteSettings()` / `saveMmLiteSettings()` — DB-backed settings (enabled flag, default TTL, phone number ID)
  - `sendMmLiteTemplate()` — Routes marketing template sends via Cloud API with `ttl.seconds` when mm_lite_enabled=true; falls back to standard path for A/B control group
  - `expireMmLiteSends()` / `startMmLiteSweeper()` — Background TTL sweeper marks sent-but-undelivered messages as expired after TTL elapses
  - `updateMmLiteDeliveryStatus()` — Webhook handler integration point for delivery confirmations
  - `queryMmLiteBenchmark()` — Aggregates delivery rates by sendApi for A/B comparison
- `src/routes/admin/mm-lite.ts`: Admin API routes
  - `GET/PUT /mm-lite/settings` — Enable/disable MM Lite, set default TTL, store phone number ID
  - `POST /mm-lite/send` — Send single recipient marketing template (with optional per-send TTL override and A/B control flag)
  - `GET /analytics/mm-lite/benchmark` — Delivery rate comparison MM Lite vs standard (configurable day window)
- `src/routes/admin/index.ts`: Registered mm-lite routes
- `src/assistant/data/settings.json`: Added `mm_lite` section documenting the feature flag and TTL defaults
- `.env.example`: Documented META_ACCESS_TOKEN, WABA_PHONE_NUMBER_ID, WABA_BUSINESS_ID env vars

### Patterns discovered
- MM Lite TTL is passed as `ttl.seconds` in the Cloud API request body (not in WhatsApp Manager)
- A/B benchmark requires tagging each send with `send_api` field ('mm_lite' | 'standard')
- `pool` (raw pg) used for DB operations to stay consistent with other lib files (pacing-monitor, marketing-optin)
- Settings stored in `app_settings` table with `mm_lite_*` prefixed keys

### Gotchas
- Cloud API credentials (META_ACCESS_TOKEN, WABA_PHONE_NUMBER_ID) are not in .env.example for existing code — added them
- Startup error (db-url.js ERR_AMBIGUOUS_MODULE_SYNTAX) is pre-existing and not caused by this change
- MM Lite requires WABA opt-in in WhatsApp Manager before routing traffic — the enabled flag serves as the guard

## Iteration [Ralph] - Story: US-1008 - Webhook message deduplication via Redis idempotency key store

### What was implemented
- Extended existing US-991 dedup in `src/lib/message-queue.ts` to satisfy US-1008 acceptance criteria:
  - Changed Redis key prefix from `rainbow:dedup:` to `whatsapp:msg:` (AC1)
  - Changed default TTL from 24h (86400s) to 4h (14400s) to bound Redis memory (AC3)
  - Added `dedupHitCount` counter that increments on every duplicate drop (AC4)
  - Dedup log now includes `totalHits=N` metric in every skip log line (AC4)
  - Added `getDedupStats()` export returning `{dedupHits, ttlSeconds, memoryDedupSize}` for admin dashboard
  - Added `dedupHits` field to `QueueHealthMetrics` interface (exposed via `/health` endpoint)
  - Added `resetDedupHitCount()` and `dedupHitCount` getter to test exports

### Tests updated
- Rewrote `src/assistant/__tests__/message-dedup.test.ts` — 13 tests covering all 5 ACs:
  - AC1: First delivery processed (new message returns false)
  - AC2: Duplicate acknowledged but skipped; no duplicate AI call
  - AC3: 4-hour TTL; expiry causes re-processing; TTL configurable
  - AC4: Hit count incremented; totalHits logged; getDedupStats() returns metrics
  - AC5: TTL expiry causes re-processing (aged entry treated as new)

### Patterns discovered
- Existing US-991 implementation was 90% of US-1008 — only needed key prefix, TTL, and metric changes
- In-memory fallback map has same TTL semantics as Redis SETNX (both expire entries)
- `getDedupStats()` can be consumed by admin dashboard or Prometheus-compatible scraper

### Build & Quality
✅ esbuild passes (no new errors)
✅ 13/13 tests pass
✅ Server startup succeeds
